## 1. Objective and non-goals

### Objective

This specification establishes the verified, primary-source research baseline as of **2026-11-02** for rewriting Lab 10 (`labs/lab10.md`, governed by `specs/labs/lab10.md`) around a **spectrum of agent memory** in the GitHub Copilot ecosystem. It supersedes the pre-GA Copilot Memory findings in `specs/001-research-sota/memory.md` (which predated the 2026-10-28 documentation overhaul and general-availability release, such as the former 28-day unused expiry in F458 and four-surface list in F462) and answers five bounded questions with verified claims (`F1`–`F575`): **(A) GitHub Copilot Memory** — the split between repository-level facts and user-level preferences, who can see, create, and delete each, how each is cited, which eight Copilot surfaces (plus VS Code and the Copilot SDK) use Memory and how memories cross surfaces, automatic versus prompt-driven creation, scope phrasing and ambiguity handling, why prompting does not guarantee storage or use and why custom instructions are the consistent mechanism, reinforcement by automatic upvoting/downvoting and current-branch citation validation, the 90-day default retention period and 7–180 day configurable repository-fact retention, content that is rejected or never stored, separate plan defaults for user-level preferences (Pro, Pro+, Max vs Business, Enterprise) and repository-level facts, billing-entity ownership and JSONL administrator export/deletion/auditing on Business and Enterprise plans, fork isolation, settings paths for viewing and deleting each memory type, why asking Copilot to "forget" only downvotes, and why the GitHub Copilot app's per-project Memory setting is a separate feature; **(B) GitHub Copilot Dreaming** — its public preview and opt-in status, how it maintains memories and repository guidance in the background without an open conversation, its three capabilities (*Repository customization*, *Personal memory curation*, and *Code review customization*) with their inputs and outputs, how changes are applied (automatic memory updates, pull requests for checked-in instructions and skills, and internal code review guidance updates), the eight tracked file patterns it can review, prerequisites (`GPT-6.1 Sol` model access, Copilot Pro and GHE.com data-residency exclusions, self-hosted runner exclusion for code review customization, and session syncing via the `Store local sessions in the Cloud` policy and `remoteExport` in `~/.copilot/settings.json`), the AI-credit cost model (billing on every run, including no-change runs and inactive repositories, plus budget and attribution caveats), how to enable or disable Dreaming at enterprise, organization, personal, and repository levels, and why turning it off does not undo prior changes; **(C) the Copilot CLI memory surface** — `/memory` (`show`, `on`, `off`), `"memory": false` in `~/.copilot/settings.json`, `--enable-memory` for non-interactive runs, interactive approval prompt options and scopes (`permissions-config.json`), the root-agent-only restriction on `store_memory` and `vote_memory` versus subagent `read_memories`, and bulk import via `copilot memories import <file>` (`--dry-run`, `--on-conflict skip`, `--output json`, the JSONL record schema, user versus repository scope shapes, import-as-approval semantics, and partial-failure retry guidance); **(D) SCNS (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns`)** — an **alpha**, local, self-hosted memory and coordination service for Copilot CLI verified against its six approved repository source files; and **(E) Evidence-backed trade-offs** comparing native Copilot Memory + Dreaming, the repository's in-repo markdown lessons wiki (`.copilot/lessons/` + `/consolidate-lessons`), and SCNS across nine operational dimensions.

### Non-goals

This specification does **not**:

- Edit `labs/lab10.md`, `docs/_meta/registry.yaml`, `docs/memory-decision-tree.md`, `solutions/lab10-session-management/sample-memories.jsonl`, or `lab-studio/`; those edits are downstream implementation work governed by `specs/labs/lab10.md`.
- Execute live state-changing operations against GitHub Copilot Memory, enable Copilot Dreaming or cloud session syncing, run `copilot memories import` without `--dry-run`, or install and start the SCNS service during this research run.
- Re-verify the non-Copilot competitor survey in `specs/001-research-sota/memory.md` (Anthropic Claude Code, Anthropic's client-side memory tool, OpenAI Codex, Cursor, Letta, or `@modelcontextprotocol/server-memory`).
- Invent benchmark numbers, token counts, schedule intervals, or AI-credit prices where primary sources are silent; every unverified, contradicted, or unsourced point is carried visibly in `§4`.
- Touch the `feature/epic-enterprise-harness` arc (Labs 21–26), which remains out of scope for Curriculum v2.

## 2. Acceptance criteria

Each criterion is **observable**: checkable by a named command or a named artifact.
A criterion no one can run is a wish, not a criterion.

Every command below is run from the repository root in PowerShell and is reproduced in full in the verification block that follows the table, keyed by its criterion number (`AC1`–`AC10`). `$SPEC` is `specs/006-copilot-memory-dreaming-scns.md`.

| # | Criterion | How it is observed |
|---|---|---|
| AC1 | This spec carries every top-level section heading (`^## `) of `specs/_template.md`, in the exact order of `specs/_template.md`, and no others | `AC1` — `Compare-Object` of the `^## ` lines in `specs/_template.md` and `$SPEC` returns `0` differences |
| AC2 | `§3` holds at least one fact row, and the rows are numbered uniquely and contiguously from `F1` to `F575`, with no gap and no repeat | `AC2` — `$rows.Count` is `575` (`-gt 0`), and `Compare-Object $n (1..$rows.Count)` returns `0` differences |
| AC3 | Every `§3` fact row carries the verdict `` `VERIFIED` `` in its fifth column, and `§3` contains no second Markdown table (no non-empty line in `§3` outside the header, separator, and fact rows contains an unescaped pipe) | `AC3` — `0` rows fail the trailing `` `VERIFIED` `` check, and the count of non-empty lines in `§3` matching `(?<!\\)\|` equals `$rows.Count + 2` |
| AC4 | Every `§3` fact row cites either one absolute `https://` URL (`387` rows) or one approved local SCNS grounding path with a `#<locator>` fragment (`188` rows) in its `Primary source URL` cell | `AC4` — `https://` row count (`387`) plus approved grounding-path row count (`188`) equals `$rows.Count` (`575`) |
| AC5 | Every fact row (`F1`–`F575`) is attributed to a surface/topic group **exactly once** in `### Surface index`, the index names all core surfaces/mechanisms (`github.com`, `VS Code`, `Copilot CLI`, `cloud agent`, `code review`, `Copilot app`, `Dreaming`, `.copilot/lessons/`, `SCNS`), and `§3` maps all six SCNS grounding files to their canonical public `https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/<path>` URLs | `AC5` — `Surface index` F-count equals `$rows.Count`, unique F-count equals `$rows.Count`, `Compare-Object` against `1..$rows.Count` is `0`, and all named surfaces and six public SCNS URLs match |
| AC6 | Each of the five sub-areas `(A)`–`(E)` in the Objective has its own subsection in `§3`, numbered `### 3.1` to `### 3.5`, and each subsection cites verified `F<digits>` rows | `AC6` — five `^### 3\.[1-5] ` headings exist and each subsection body matches `F\d+` |
| AC7 | Every `F<digits>` token cited in `### Verifier caveats` and in `### 3.1`–`### 3.5` exists in the `§3` fact table (`1..575`), and every `Q<digits>` token cited in `§3` exists in `§4` (`1..16`) | `AC7` — `0` narrative or caveat `F`-numbers fall outside `1..$rows.Count`, and `0` cited `Q`-numbers fall outside `1..$q.Count` |
| AC8 | Subsections `3.1`–`3.5` explicitly cover every required sub-topic of `(A)` Copilot Memory, `(B)` Copilot Dreaming, `(C)` Copilot CLI memory surface, `(D)` SCNS (marked `alpha`), and `(E)` the nine trade-off dimensions (`Where data lives`, `Who can see it`, `Governance and administrator control`, `Determinism (advisory vs binding)`, `Inspectability and diff-ability`, `Cost model`, `Cross-surface reach`, `Offline and local operation`, `Isolation`) | `AC8` — per-subsection regex assertions in the PowerShell check block return no missing-topic output |
| AC9 | `### Source currency` and `### Source concentration` state the undated/unattributed-date row count (`245`), the distinct exact source-string count (`168`), the largest single exact-source row count (`73`), the `https://` row count (`387`), and the local-grounding row count (`188`), and all five match the values recomputed from the `§3` table | `AC9` — recomputed `$undated`, `$d`, `$top`, `$httpsCount`, and `$localCount` all match in their respective subsections |
| AC10 | `§4` carries `Q` rows numbered uniquely and contiguously from `Q1`, and every `Q` row explicitly names its grade (`CONTRADICTED`, `UNVERIFIED`, or `NO-SOURCE`) | `AC10` — `Compare-Object $qn (1..$q.Count)` is `0`, and `0` rows omit the three valid grades |

```powershell
# Run from the repository root.
$SPEC = 'specs/006-copilot-memory-dreaming-scns.md'
$raw  = Get-Content $SPEC -Raw -Encoding UTF8
$rows = Select-String -Path $SPEC -Pattern '^\| F(\d+) \|'
$n    = $rows | ForEach-Object { [int]$_.Matches[0].Groups[1].Value }

# AC1 — template conformance, order-sensitive
(Compare-Object (Select-String -Path 'specs/_template.md' -Pattern '^## ').Line `
                (Select-String -Path $SPEC -Pattern '^## ').Line -SyncWindow 0).Count  # expect 0

# AC2 — fact rows numbered uniquely and contiguously from F1
$rows.Count -eq 575                                                                    # expect True
(Compare-Object $n (1..$rows.Count)).Count                                             # expect 0

# AC3 — every fact row ends in `VERIFIED`, and §3 contains no other unescaped-pipe lines
($rows | Where-Object { $_.Line -notmatch '\| `VERIFIED` \|\s*$' }).Count              # expect 0
$sec3Lines = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^## 3\. Verified facts\r?\n(.*?)(?=^## 4\. )').Groups[1].Value -split '\r?\n'
($sec3Lines | Where-Object { $_.Trim() -ne '' -and $_ -match '(?<!\\)\|' }).Count -eq ($rows.Count + 2)  # expect True

# AC4 — every fact row cites either an https:// URL (387) or an approved SCNS grounding file with #locator (188)
$httpsCount = (Select-String -Path $SPEC -Pattern '^\| F\d+ \|.+ \| https://[^ |]+ \| ').Count
$localCount = (Select-String -Path $SPEC -Pattern '^\| F\d+ \|.+ \| C:/Users/johnhain/\.copilot/session-state/[^|]+#[^|]+ \| ').Count
$httpsCount -eq 387                                                                    # expect True
$localCount -eq 188                                                                    # expect True
($httpsCount + $localCount) -eq $rows.Count                                            # expect True

# AC5 — every fact row attributed to a surface/group in Surface index exactly once + SCNS public URLs present
$idx = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^### Surface index(.*?)^### 3\.1 ').Groups[1].Value
$f   = [regex]::Matches($idx, 'F(\d+)') | ForEach-Object { [int]$_.Groups[1].Value }
$f.Count -eq $rows.Count                                                               # expect True
($f | Sort-Object -Unique).Count -eq $rows.Count                                       # expect True
(Compare-Object $f $n).Count                                                           # expect 0
'github.com','VS Code','Copilot CLI','cloud agent','code review','Copilot app','Dreaming','.copilot/lessons/','SCNS' |
  ForEach-Object { if ($idx -notmatch [regex]::Escape($_)) { "MISSING SURFACE: $_" } } # expect no output
'https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md',
'https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/NORTH_STAR.md',
'https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/PRIOR_ART.md',
'https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/modules.md',
'https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/copilot-integration.md',
'https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/src/copilot-extension/tool-definitions.ts' |
  ForEach-Object { if ($raw -notmatch [regex]::Escape($_)) { "MISSING SCNS URL: $_" } } # expect no output

# AC6 — five sub-area subsections (3.1 to 3.5), each citing verified F-rows
(Select-String -Path $SPEC -Pattern '^### 3\.[1-5] ').Count                            # expect 5
1..5 | ForEach-Object {
  $b = [regex]::Match($raw, "(?sm)^### 3\.$_ (.*?)(?=^### 3\.|^## 4\. )").Groups[1].Value
  if ($b -notmatch 'F\d+') { "UNSUPPORTED: 3.$_" }
}                                                                                      # expect no output

# AC7 — every F-number and Q-number cited in §3 exists in §3 / §4
$q  = Select-String -Path $SPEC -Pattern '^\| Q(\d+) \|'
$qn = $q | ForEach-Object { [int]$_.Matches[0].Groups[1].Value }
$nar = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^### Verifier caveats(.*?)^## 4\. ').Groups[1].Value
([regex]::Matches($nar, 'F(\d+)') | ForEach-Object { [int]$_.Groups[1].Value } |
   Where-Object { $n -notcontains $_ }).Count                                          # expect 0
([regex]::Matches($nar, 'Q(\d+)') | ForEach-Object { [int]$_.Groups[1].Value } |
   Where-Object { $qn -notcontains $_ }).Count                                         # expect 0

# AC8 — required sub-topic coverage in 3.1 through 3.5
$s31 = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^### 3\.1 (.*?)^### 3\.2 ').Groups[1].Value
$s32 = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^### 3\.2 (.*?)^### 3\.3 ').Groups[1].Value
$s33 = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^### 3\.3 (.*?)^### 3\.4 ').Groups[1].Value
$s34 = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^### 3\.4 (.*?)^### 3\.5 ').Groups[1].Value
$s35 = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^### 3\.5 (.*?)^## 4\. ').Groups[1].Value
'repository-level facts','user-level preferences','90 days','7 and 180 days','https://github.com/settings/copilot/memory','billing entity','downvote' |
  ForEach-Object { if ($s31 -notmatch [regex]::Escape($_)) { "MISSING 3.1: $_" } }     # expect no output
'public preview','Repository customization','Personal memory curation','Code review customization','GPT-6.1 Sol','Store local sessions in the Cloud','remoteExport','AI credits' |
  ForEach-Object { if ($s32 -notmatch [regex]::Escape($_)) { "MISSING 3.2: $_" } }     # expect no output
'/memory','--enable-memory','store_memory','vote_memory','read_memories','copilot memories import','--dry-run','--on-conflict skip','--output json','externalId' |
  ForEach-Object { if ($s33 -notmatch [regex]::Escape($_)) { "MISSING 3.3: $_" } }     # expect no output
'alpha','127.0.0.1:3456','3500','vault.db','FTS5','sqlite-vec','384','3,400','21 tools','--context-injection','scns memory prune','experience.db','SCNS_UNATTENDED' |
  ForEach-Object { if ($s34 -notmatch [regex]::Escape($_)) { "MISSING 3.4: $_" } }     # expect no output
'Where data lives','Who can see it','Governance and administrator control','Determinism (advisory vs binding)','Inspectability and diff-ability','Cost model','Cross-surface reach','Offline and local operation','Isolation' |
  ForEach-Object { if ($s35 -notmatch [regex]::Escape($_)) { "MISSING 3.5: $_" } }     # expect no output

# AC9 — source currency and source concentration integers match recomputed table values
$cur = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^### Source currency(.*?)^### Source concentration').Groups[1].Value
$con = [regex]::Match($raw, '(?sm)^### Source concentration(.*?)^### Surface index').Groups[1].Value
$undated = ($rows | Where-Object { ($_.Line -split ' (?<!\\)\| ')[3] -match '(?i)undated|not stated|unknown' }).Count
$srcs = $rows | ForEach-Object { ($_.Line -split ' (?<!\\)\| ')[2].Trim() }
$d    = ($srcs | Sort-Object -Unique).Count
$top  = ($srcs | Group-Object | Sort-Object Count -Descending | Select-Object -First 1).Count
$cur -match "\b$undated\b"                                                             # expect True
$con -match "\b$d\b"                                                                   # expect True
$con -match "\b$top\b"                                                                 # expect True
$con -match "\b$httpsCount\b"                                                          # expect True
$con -match "\b$localCount\b"                                                          # expect True

# AC10 — open questions numbered contiguously from Q1 and each graded
(Compare-Object $qn (1..$q.Count)).Count                                               # expect 0
($q | Where-Object { $_.Line -notmatch 'CONTRADICTED|UNVERIFIED|NO-SOURCE' }).Count    # expect 0
```

## 3. Verified facts

Every factual claim this spec relies on. **Mandatory** — a spec with no factual claims
states that explicitly rather than omitting the table.

A search-engine summary is a lead, not a source. The verifier opens the primary page.

The table below is the verified record: **575 claim rows** (`F1`–`F575`), each graded `VERIFIED` after an independent verifier opened the cited primary source. **Row count is not source count.** Those 575 rows rest on **168 distinct exact source strings** (`56` distinct base pages/files once section anchors are grouped): **387 rows** cite public `https://` web URLs and **188 rows** cite the six approved local grounding files from the `johnhain-msft/scns` repository, with **73 rows** citing the single unanchored URL `https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/prompting-and-context/copilot-memory` (`91` rows across that page including section anchors, and `130` rows across the SCNS `README.md` grounding file across its section locators). Because the research was decomposed into independent sub-questions, overlapping propositions were graded independently across batches rather than deduplicated; the **Source concentration** subsection below gives the exact breakdown and maps each SCNS grounding file to its canonical public URL (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/<path>`).

Immediately after the table are four navigation and safety aids — **Verifier caveats** (one line per entry whose verifier note narrows, qualifies, or scopes the claim), **Source currency** (date coverage and the count of undated rows), **Source concentration** (source distribution and the SCNS grounding-file to public-URL table-free mapping), and **Surface index** (assigning every row `F1`–`F575` to its surface or subsystem exactly once) — followed by **subsections 3.1 to 3.5**, which synthesize sub-areas `(A)` through `(E)` strictly from the verified rows.

A verdict of `VERIFIED` means the cited primary source was opened and states the claim at the scope recorded. It does **not** mean a vendor's marketing or benchmark figure was independently reproduced, that a documented click-path or CLI recipe was executed against a live account in this run, or that a page-scoped silence proves no guidance exists anywhere else in a vendor's corpus.

### Source currency

Today is **2026-11-02**. Every row in the table above states the basis for its date in its `Published` cell, so currency can be audited directly from the table.

- **Sources older than twelve months (published before 2025-11-02) — `0` rows.** Every dated web page, GitHub Changelog post, GitHub release note, repository commit, and dated documentation section in the table was published or committed in **2026** (spanning `2026-01-15` at F84 and F104 through `2026-11-02` at F270 and F271).
- **Superseded 2026 sources retained strictly as historical baseline comparisons — `13` rows.** Rows F84 (`2026-01-15`), F85 (`2026-03-04`), F86 (`2026-10-19` commit `b4ab111fb6`), F104 (`2026-01-15`), F105 (`2026-03-04`), F106 (`2026-05-15`), F107 (`2026-06-02`), F109 (`2026-10-19`), F110 (`2026-10-19`), and the `2026-10-09` rows from `specs/001-research-sota/memory.md` (F458, F462, F463, F464, F466) document how Copilot Memory was described during public preview prior to the **2026-10-28** general-availability release and documentation overhaul (`github/docs` commit `8e7396e5e2e0`, PR `#63873`, F1, F2, F108, F300). They are cited in `3.1`–`3.2` only to contrast the retired 28-day / single-policy / four-surface preview baseline against current November 2026 behavior.
- **Rows whose `Published` cell records the source as undated, not stated, or unknown — `245` of the `575` rows.** A row is counted here whenever its `Published` cell matches `undated`, `not stated`, or `unknown` (case-insensitively), so the integer `245` is recomputable directly from the table (`AC9`). This count comprises two groups: (1) **182 SCNS grounding rows** whose local markdown or TypeScript snapshots carry no document-level publication timestamp (only the six dated sections in `docs/PRIOR_ART.md` and `docs/modules.md` — F361, F362, F363 dated `2026-07-07`, F368 and F417 dated `2026-08-15`, and F416 noting a `2026-08-02` deletion — carry explicit dates), and (2) **63 GitHub Docs rows** where the rendered `docs.github.com` page displays no on-page date and the verifier recorded either the retrieval date (`2026-11-02`) or the latest `github/docs` source commit timestamp (`2026-10-28T15:36:59Z`, commit `8e7396e5e2e0`, or `2026-10-20T16:30:53Z`, commit `4dc0b8276c24`).
- **Provisional features as of 2026-11-02.** While GitHub Copilot Memory is now generally available on paid Copilot plans (F108, F135, F461), **GitHub Copilot Dreaming** is in **public preview and subject to change** (F133, F134, F172), **GitHub Copilot CLI extensions** are marked **experimental and subject to change** (F573), and **SCNS** is explicitly **alpha** and unverified outside the author's machines (F303, F312, F450, F451).

### 3.1 (A) GitHub Copilot Memory

**General availability and documentation provenance.** GitHub launched agentic memory for Copilot in public preview on 2026-01-15 (F84, F104), turned it on by default for Copilot Pro and Pro+ users in public preview on 2026-03-04 (F85, F105), added early-access user-level preferences for Pro and Pro+ on 2026-05-15 (F106), expanded deletion, scope, and CLI controls on 2026-05-26 (F131), rolled out user-level preferences in public preview for Copilot Business and Enterprise on 2026-06-02 (F107, F121), and announced on **2026-10-28** that **GitHub Copilot Memory is generally available on paid Copilot plans** (F108, F135, F461). On the same date (`2026-10-28`), `github/docs` commit `8e7396e5e2e0` (titled `[DO NOT MERGE] October 28-29: GitHub Universe 2026 docs-internal feature branch (#63873)`) updated `copilot-memory.md` and added `create-memories.md`, superseding the `2026-10-19` commit `b4ab111fb6` (F1, F2, F86, F109, F110, F300; see Q1, Q5, Q6) and retiring the earlier public-preview record in `specs/001-research-sota/memory.md` (F458, F462, F463, F466). Copilot Memory is available on all paid Copilot plans, and whether it is used in a given interaction depends on the plan's settings, repository access, and the surface used (F18, F88, F477).

**The two memory types: repository-level facts versus user-level preferences.** Copilot Memory stores two distinct types of memory that are stored separately and owned and managed differently (F3, F12):

- **What each captures:** *Repository-level facts* describe a codebase and are shared with collaborators — capturing coding conventions, architectural decisions, build and test commands, and project-specific rules (for example, `"this repository uses Vitest for unit tests"`) (F3, F4, F11). *User-level preferences* describe how an individual user personally likes to work and follow that user across repositories — capturing clearly stated, non-sensitive preferences, how responses should be presented, actions that require user approval, and reusable workflows across projects (for example, `"Explain your changes before you apply them"`) (F3, F4, F11).
- **Where each applies:** Repository-level facts apply **only in operations on the same repository** and are **not pooled** across an organization's repositories (F5, F12, F67, F507). User-level preferences apply in that user's Copilot interactions **across all of the user's repositories** (F3, F5, F11).
- **Who can see each:** Repository-level facts can be used by any user with read access to the repository who can use Copilot Memory; viewing the list of stored facts in the repository's settings requires **write access to the repository and user-level preferences enabled for the viewer's account** (including permission from any applicable enterprise or organization policy) (F6, F127, F128, F472; note that Microsoft's VS Code documentation page at F132 and F474 states `"With repository administration access, open the repository's Settings > Copilot > Memory"`, which is narrower than GitHub's product documentation — see F132 and F474 caveats and Q13). User-level preferences can be seen by the user and by anyone who can view a shared agent session in which the preference appears; on Copilot Business and Copilot Enterprise plans, an organization or enterprise administrator can also view and export them (F7, F114, F473).
- **Who can create each:** Repository-level facts can be created only when Copilot is working in a repository where the user has **write access** (specifically **push permission** for new repository facts to be created, F43, F130) and Copilot Memory is enabled for both the user and the repository (including organization and enterprise repository memory access settings) (F8, F17, F42, F43, F130, F472). User-level preferences can be created **only by the user**, from that user's own interactions, when Copilot can identify the user and applicable settings allow user-level preferences (F8, F17, F130, F473).
- **Who can delete each:** Repository-level facts can be deleted by users with **write access** to the repository who have **user-level preferences enabled** for their account (F9, F127, F128, F298, F472; vs VS Code's narrower administrator phrasing at F132, F474 — see Q13). User-level preferences can be deleted by the user, and — on Copilot Business and Copilot Enterprise plans — by an organization or enterprise administrator (F9, F15, F114, F115, F473).
- **How each is cited:** Repository-level facts are stored **with citations pointing to the code that supports the fact** (F10, F293). User-level preferences are stored **with citations that may include direct quotes of what the user said** (F10, F33, F293).
- **Stored metadata versus what is displayed in GitHub.com settings:** For each memory, GitHub stores memory text, subject area, agent, model, billing entity, citations, reason for storing, whether it is a repository fact or user preference, voting data, and expiry datetime (F16, F80, F492). Only **memory text, subject area, agent, model, and billing entity** (when present; an organization billing entity is shown with a building icon and an enterprise billing entity with a globe icon) are displayed in the GitHub.com settings pages; **citations, reason for storing, the repository-fact-versus-user-preference indicator, voting data, and expiry datetime are not displayed**, and internal fields such as voting details are neither shown on the website nor included in audit logs (F16, F80, F81, F492).

**Which Copilot surfaces use Memory, per-surface defaults, and cross-surface sharing.** The `Where Copilot Memory is available` section of the concept page lists **eight surfaces** that can use Copilot Memory: **GitHub Copilot app, Copilot CLI, Copilot code review, Copilot cloud agent, Agentic Autofix, Copilot in GitHub Mobile, Copilot in Slack, and Copilot in Microsoft Teams** (F19, F498; expanded from four surfaces in the pre-GA concept page recorded at F462). Although **Visual Studio Code** is omitted from that eight-bullet list, the same page's `Settings for each surface` table and `What determines whether Copilot Memory is used` section include a `Visual Studio Code` row and state that VS Code has its own application setting (`chat.copilot.memory.enabled`) for Copilot sessions in its Local Agent Host (F21, F99). GitHub's `Settings for each surface` table gives the following defaults, each operating only when account, repository, and policy settings also allow Memory (F22, F97, F499):

- **Copilot CLI interactive sessions:** `Local setting on. Follows your GitHub settings.` (F22, F28, F97, F98, F237, F242, F499).
- **Copilot CLI programmatic / non-interactive sessions (`copilot -p`):** `Off. Use --enable-memory for each run.` (F22, F28, F97, F98, F238, F242, F255, F499).
- **Visual Studio Code:** GitHub Docs state `Off. Enable the local setting.` (`chat.copilot.memory.enabled` off by default even when GitHub settings allow memory, F21, F22, F97, F99, F499), whereas Microsoft's VS Code documentation (`code.visualstudio.com`, last updated 2026-10-28) states that cloud-backed Copilot Memory is **on by default in VS Code** (`chat.copilot.memory.enabled` default `true`) and uses the same cloud memory service for GitHub-hosted repositories (F100, F101, F469, F500; see F100, F101, F500 caveats and Q13).
- **GitHub Copilot app:** `Follows your GitHub settings, where available for your account.` Availability in the app depends on whether Copilot Memory has been made available for the account; there is no toggle in the app to turn on availability and no screen in the app to manage shared memories (F22, F25, F97, F126, F499).
- **Copilot cloud agent (including sessions started from Slack or Microsoft Teams):** `Follows your GitHub settings.` (F22, F24, F97, F499).
- **Copilot code review and Agentic Autofix:** `Follows repository settings.` (F22, F97, F499).
- **Copilot SDK applications:** Can enable Copilot Memory when creating or resuming a session, but the SDK provides no APIs to list, edit, or delete memories (F27).

**Cross-surface reach and surface-specific scope/approval rules.** Facts and preferences captured by one Copilot surface **can be used by another Copilot surface** — for example, if Copilot cloud agent discovers how a repository handles database connections, Copilot code review can apply that fact when reviewing a pull request, and if Copilot code review learns that settings must stay synchronized across two files, Copilot cloud agent knows to update both files when changing one (F20, F498). All of these surfaces support repository-level facts, and all **except Copilot code review and Agentic Autofix** also support user-level preferences; code review and agentic autofix **never apply user-level preferences**, so personal preferences do not alter how pull requests are reviewed or how security alerts are fixed (in Copilot CLI, user preferences belong to the user who started the session) (F23, F244, F501). In the **GitHub Copilot app** and **Copilot CLI interactive sessions**, Copilot can ask the user to approve storing a memory, whereas **Copilot cloud agent** (including sessions started from Slack or Microsoft Teams) and **Copilot code review** do not ask for approval (F24, F245, F501).

**Boundary with the GitHub Copilot app's per-project Memory setting.** The `Memory` setting for a project inside the GitHub Copilot app is a **separate feature**: it stores preferences for that project across tasks, not the shared repository-level facts and user-level preferences of Copilot Memory, and editing or turning off a project's memory in the app does not edit or turn off Copilot Memory (F26, F126, F502).

**How memories are created, scope phrasing, and why custom instructions are the consistent mechanism.** When Copilot Memory is enabled, Copilot creates memories **automatically as a byproduct of working with you**, noticing durable repository facts and personal preferences that it judges worth keeping (F29, F30). Users **cannot create or edit an individual memory in the settings pages on GitHub.com**; instead, they ask Copilot to add the memory via prompt in Copilot CLI, Copilot cloud agent, the GitHub Copilot app, or Visual Studio Code (or import prepared memories in bulk with Copilot CLI; the `Asking Copilot to remember a fact or preference` section links prompting guides for Copilot cloud agent, Copilot CLI, and Copilot code review, and notes that pull request comments must start with `@copilot` to trigger Copilot cloud agent as a prompt) (F31, F32, F37, F130, F493). If a user clearly states a preference or repository fact or explicitly asks Copilot to remember something, it is *likely* to be captured, with the user's words recorded as the citation, and if a matching memory already exists Copilot can reinforce it rather than creating a duplicate (F33, F34, F42).

- **Model decides scope; explicit phrasing guidance; ambiguity prompts clarification:** Copilot chooses whether to store a memory as a repository-level fact or a user-level preference based on **who the memory applies to**, rather than who stated it (F34, F38, F39). Something true for anyone working in the repository becomes a repository-level fact (phrase as `"Something that is true for anyone working in this repository"`, e.g., `"In this repository, database migrations are always run before the test suite."`, `"Store a memory that api/openapi.gen.go is generated here. Edit openapi.yaml instead."`, `"Remember that API handlers in this repository return errors through formatApiError."`, or `"Create a memory for this repository: Database migrations belong in db/migrations, not in application startup code."`), whereas something reflecting how the user personally likes to work across projects becomes a user-level preference (phrase as `"Something about how you personally like to work, across all your projects"`, e.g., `"In all my projects, I prefer that you explain a change before applying it."`, `"Remember I always want the smallest change that fixes the problem, not a refactor."`, `"Across all my projects, remember to ask for my approval before publishing changes."`, or `"Remember my workflow for all projects: Run the relevant tests and summarize their results before proposing a commit."`) (F35, F36, F38, F40). Users must **not** rely on words such as `"always"` or `"going forward"` to imply a scope; they should state explicitly whether the memory applies to them across repositories or to everyone working in the current repository (F38, F39, F41). **If the intended scope is ambiguous, Copilot asks the user to clarify rather than guessing** (F38, F39).
- **Prompting does not guarantee storage or use; custom instructions are the consistent mechanism:** Prompting Copilot to store a memory **does not guarantee that it will be stored** (the model decides based on the prompt and context), and even when a memory is stored, **Copilot is not guaranteed to send or apply it in every session** (F49, F51, F52, F57, F488, F489, F490). Because memory selection and validation are judgments made by Copilot rather than system-enforced rules, GitHub Docs state explicitly: **"To provide instructions consistently, use custom instructions rather than relying on Copilot Memory"** — using custom instructions (such as `.github/copilot-instructions.md` or `AGENTS.md`, which are written, maintained, and reviewed by humans) for important rules like security requirements or mandatory review steps, while letting Copilot Memory handle accumulated detail that would make instruction files long and hard to maintain (noting also that neither memories nor custom instructions guarantee deterministic AI compliance every time, so outputs must still be reviewed) (F49, F51, F52, F53, F488, F489, F490, F491).

**Reinforcement, automatic voting, current-branch citation checks, and retention/expiry.** Copilot does not treat a stored memory as permanently true (F54):

- **Citation validation against the current branch:** Before using a memory, Copilot is instructed to check its citations (F54). When Copilot finds a relevant **repository-level fact**, it **checks the fact's citations against the current branch** to judge whether the information is still accurate (including repository facts captured from pull requests that were closed without merging, though checking against the current codebase does not guarantee recognizing every outdated fact) (F54, F55, F62). When Copilot finds a relevant **user-level preference**, it uses its best judgment to confirm the preference still applies (the concept page specifies a current-branch check only for repository-level facts, F56).
- **Automatic upvoting and downvoting:** Copilot uses an internal voting system automatically to rate each memory's usefulness — **upvoting** a memory it is able to validate and **downvoting** a memory found to be wrong or outdated (F54, F58). Users can influence voting by giving a positive or negative comment about the use of a memory during a session (F58). Not all stored memories are sent with a prompt; upvoted memories are more likely to be sent and rise up the list on the GitHub.com settings pages, while downvoted memories move down the list (F57, F58). Memories downvoted past a threshold are no longer used, are **hidden from the settings list** (though they can remain stored until they expire, so disappearing from the list does not necessarily mean a memory has been deleted), and are deleted after a period of disuse (F57, F58, F59, F79; see Q2).
- **Retention and expiry (90-day default; 7–180 days configurable for repository-level facts):** Stored repository-level facts and user-level preferences expire after a period without reinforcement (F60, F69, F72, F73, F459, F479). The **default expiry period is 90 days** (F60, F70, F73, F459, F479), replacing the 28-day unused expiry described in early 2026 preview announcements and pre-2026-10-28 documentation (F84, F85, F86, F458; note that Microsoft's VS Code documentation page as of 2026-10-28 still states 28 days, F87, F460 — see Q13). The expiry period starts when a memory is created and **resets to the default or configured period only when Copilot upvotes it**; **simply retrieving a memory does not reset the period**, and **downvoting a memory does not alter the remaining time before it expires** (F60, F69, F73, F76, F459). **Enterprise owners** (via Enterprise > `AI controls` > `Copilot` > `Features & clients` > `Configure features & clients` > `Features` > `Copilot Memory retention` > `Save`, F77) and **organization owners** (via organization `Settings` > `Copilot` > `Policies` > `Features` > `Copilot Memory retention` > `Save retention period`, F78) can configure the expiry period for **repository-level facts** to any value between **7 and 180 days (inclusive)** (F61, F71, F73, F459, F479). This setting **does not apply to user-level preferences**, which always use the default 90-day retention period; changing the retention period does not re-date already-stored repository facts (they adopt the new period the next time Copilot reinforces them), and if both an organization and its enterprise configure a period, the **lower (shorter) of the two values** is used (F61, F72, F74, F75, F459, F479).

**What Copilot never stores.** The concept page separates content blocked by the system from content Copilot is instructed not to store (F63, F485):

1. **Secrets (rejected by the system):** Memories are scanned for secrets such as tokens, keys, or credentials; memories in which a secret is detected are **rejected and are not stored** (F63, F485).
2. **Model-control tokens (rejected by the system):** The system can **reject** memories containing recognized model-control tokens as a defense against prompt injection (F64, F485).
3. **Sensitive or confidential information (instruction to Copilot):** Copilot is instructed not to store sensitive personal information, confidential information, or anything shared in confidence (F65, F485).
4. **Short-lived instructions (instruction to Copilot):** Copilot is instructed not to store short-lived or task-specific instructions, because memories are intended for facts and preferences that stay true over time (F66, F485).

**Default enablement per plan and the separate default for repository-level facts.** User-level preferences and repository-level facts have **separate defaults and independent controls** (F48, F89, F92, F95, F96, F102, F189, F297, F477; superseding the pre-2026-10-28 single-policy docs at F109, F110, F463):

- **User-level preferences default by plan:** On **Copilot Pro, Copilot Pro+, and Copilot Max**, user-level preferences are **Enabled** by default, and users can opt out in personal settings (profile picture > `Copilot settings` > `Features` > `Copilot Memory`; existing opt-outs remain in effect) (F48, F89, F102, F189, F477). On **Copilot Business and Copilot Enterprise**, user-level preferences are **Disabled** by default until an organization owner or enterprise owner enables the Copilot Memory policy; once enabled, storage and use of user-level preferences are on by default for members who have not opted out, and members can opt out individually without an admin policy change clearing their personal or local client opt-out (F48, F90, F91, F189, F297, F477). If a user receives Copilot subscriptions from more than one organization, the **most restrictive** user-memory policy applies (user-level preferences are not used unless *all* of those organizations allow them) (F93). At the enterprise level (Enterprise > `AI controls` > `Copilot` > `Configure features & clients` > `Features` > `Copilot Memory`), the three policy options for user-level preferences are `Let organizations decide` (off by default for all orgs; org owners can enable), `Enabled everywhere` (enabled for all orgs; org owners cannot disable; individual users can still opt out), and `Disabled everywhere` (disabled for all orgs; org owners cannot enable) (F94).
- **Separate default for repository-level facts:** For all of these paid plans (F48, F477; see Q3), **repository memory access is allowed unless enterprise, organization, or repository settings restrict it**, and an organization's `Repository access` dropdown (at organization `Settings` > `Copilot` > `Policies` > `Features` > `Copilot Memory`, with options `No repositories`, `All repositories`, and `Selected repositories`) defaults to **`All repositories`** (F48, F92, F96, F189, F477, F478). An organization can allow user-level preferences without repository-level facts, or repository-level facts without user-level preferences (F92, F96). Disabling user-level preferences in personal settings **does not disable repository-level facts** or delete stored memories (F48, F102), and disabling both memory types for an enterprise requires disabling both user-level preferences and repository memory access (F95). At the repository level, only **repository administrators** can toggle `Allow Copilot to store and retrieve repository facts` under repository `Settings` > `Copilot` > `Memory`; turning it off stops Copilot from creating or applying repository-level facts in that repository, leaves already-stored repository facts unused but not deleted, and does not affect user-level preferences (F129, F131, F478, F510).

**Business/Enterprise billing-entity ownership, multi-license default billing entity, admin export/delete, and auditing.** On Copilot Business and Copilot Enterprise plans, **user-level preferences are owned by the billing entity** (the organization or enterprise that grants the user their license) (F13, F111, F121, F508). Each preference is stored against the user's active billing entity at the time of creation, its owner never changes afterwards, and when Copilot builds context for an agent session it **retrieves only preferences owned by the user's current active billing entity** — if a user later moves to a different license, earlier preferences stay with the prior organization or enterprise and are no longer retrieved for that user (F13, F111, F112, F508, F509). Users who receive Copilot licenses from multiple enterprises or organizations **must select a default billing entity** in their account settings (`https://github.com/settings/copilot/features`) before Copilot can create new repository-level facts or user-level preferences for them (F14, F103, F124). Repository-level facts are **not** owned by a billing entity; they belong to the repository in which they were created and remain associated with that repository regardless of changes to an individual user's license (F112, F113, F509).

- **Administrator JSONL export and permanent deletion:** Enterprise and organization owners can export (in **JSONL** format) or permanently delete user-level preferences for users whose Copilot seats are billed to them and whose preferences were generated with that organization or enterprise as the active billing entity, either **in bulk or per user** (F15, F115, F116, F118, F121, F481). Clicking `Download memories` confirms administrator permission to access users' memories; exports are reusable for up to **2 hours** (so repeated downloads within that window may not reflect recent changes), only the administrator who requested an export can check its status or download it, another administrator cannot request the same bulk or per-user export while a reusable export exists, and creating new exports is rate-limited to **10 per hour** per enterprise (or per organization for organization exports, with reused downloads not counting toward the limit) (F116, F117, F481). Administrator deletion displays a confirmation dialog listing the affected users and, once `Delete memories` is clicked, is **permanent and cannot be undone** (F118, F481). The organization navigation path is organization `Settings` > `Copilot` (under `Code, planning, and automation`) > `Access`, using the kebab menu next to a user (`Export Copilot memories` or `Delete Copilot memories`) or the `Get usage report` dropdown (`Download user memories` or `Delete user memories`) (F119). The enterprise navigation path is enterprise > `Billing and licensing` > `Licensing` > `Copilot` section `Manage`, using the `Open seat options` kebab menu per user or the `Get reports` dropdown in bulk (all user-level preferences can also be downloaded from `AI Controls` > `Copilot` > `Configure features & clients`) (F120).
- **Audit log events:** Memory lifecycle entries are written to the organization or enterprise audit log when a memory is **created or deleted** (searchable with `action:copilot.memory` for user-level activity, `action:org.copilot_memory` for organization-owner activity, `action:business.copilot_memory` for enterprise-owner activity, and `action:copilot.memory_created repo:ORG/REPO` for repository memory creation), and administrator exports generate audit entries when requested, completed, and downloaded; **retrieving a memory, voting on it, or its automatic expiry does not create a memory lifecycle audit event**, and audit entries **never contain a memory's content, citations, or reason for creation or deletion** (F82, F122, F480; superseding the pre-2026-10-28 audit description in Spec 001 at F466).

**Forks do not copy memories.** Each repository-level fact belongs to the one specific repository it was created in and applies only in operations on that same repository: **forking a repository does not copy its stored memories to the fork**, though the user's own user-level preferences still apply when working in the fork (F5, F12, F67, F113, F468, F507). (For Lab 10, because forking does not copy stored memories to the fork and repository-level facts belong to the repository they were created in and apply only in operations on that same repository, a learner working in their own fork does not inherit upstream repository-level facts and any repository-level fact created in the fork belongs only to the fork, F5, F12, F67, F113, F468, F507.)

**Where memories are viewed and deleted, and why `"forget"` only downvotes.** Users view and delete their own **user-level preferences** in personal settings at **`https://github.com/settings/copilot/memory`** (profile picture > `Copilot settings` > under `Copilot` in the sidebar click `Memory` > click the trash icon next to a single memory, or select multiple checkboxes and click `Delete`) (F14, F123, F124, F131, F132, F298, F468). Users with **write access to a repository and user-level preferences enabled for their account** view and delete **repository-level facts** at **repository `Settings` > `Copilot` (under `Code, planning, and automation`) > `Memory`**, using the trash icon or selecting checkboxes and clicking `Delete` (F6, F9, F12, F127, F128, F131, F298, F468, F472; see F132, F474 and Q13 on VS Code's narrower administrator phrasing). **Copilot cannot delete a memory in response to a prompt:** asking Copilot to `"forget"` a memory can **downvote** that fact or preference, but does **not** delete it from the memory store; immediate deletion must be performed in personal or repository settings (F50, F59, F83, F251, F299, F487).

**Usage and billing for Copilot Memory.** Memories included in a model's context count toward token usage and can affect the AI credits consumed by an interaction, with the cost depending on the model and the number of tokens used (F68, F495). In the 2026-10-28 changelog post, GitHub reported that among reviewed public-preview Copilot CLI sessions where the agent used memory, `93%` showed better adherence to developer preferences and `42%` showed more efficient workflows attributed to memories in context, without disclosing sample size, baseline, or methodology (F152, F511; see Q15). The Copilot Memory concept page states no maximum memory count or size limit, no storage region or encryption detail, and does not mention offline operation (F512; see Q16).

### 3.2 (B) GitHub Copilot Dreaming

**Public preview status and opt-in posture.** Announced on **2026-10-28** alongside the general availability of Copilot Memory (and framing *GitHub intelligence* as three capabilities: `"Memory carries knowledge between sessions"`, `"Dreaming keeps the context current"`, and `"Code search reaches beyond your checkout"`, F136), **GitHub Copilot Dreaming is in public preview and is subject to change** (F133, F134, F135, F172, F173, F461, F465; superseding Spec 001 F126's pre-Dreaming statement at F464). Dreaming is **opt-in and disabled by default** across personal accounts, personally owned repositories, organizations, and enterprises (F137, F138, F139, F214, F220, F482).

**What Copilot Dreaming does and how it runs.** Copilot Dreaming works in the background to keep Copilot's memories and repository guidance up to date: it analyzes eligible previously synced Copilot sessions, memories, repository content, and pull request review feedback to identify useful information, correct outdated guidance (for example, proposing an update when a repository's instructions describe an obsolete test command), and remove duplicate memories, using only some of this information on any given run (F134, F140, F141, F146, F198, F465, F471). Copilot Memory continues to store and reuse repository facts and personal preferences during normal interactions even when Dreaming is off; Dreaming maintains those memories in the background and **updates context, not the underlying AI model** (F141, F465). **You do not need to keep a Copilot conversation open for Copilot Dreaming to run** (F168, F229, F505). Dreaming runs automatically on a **schedule managed by GitHub**; a run might not appear immediately after enabling it, and users and administrators **cannot start runs manually or edit their schedules, prompts, or the model used by Copilot Dreaming** (F160, F227, F483).

**The three Dreaming capabilities, their inputs, and their outputs.** Each Dreaming setting enables all capabilities whose prerequisites are met within its scope (for repositories, this can include both repository customization and code review customization), and **you cannot switch these capabilities on or off individually** (F148, F188). The `What Dreaming can do` table and the 2026-10-28 changelog define three dream types (F143, F144, F145, F149, F150, F151, F503):

1. **Repository customization:**
   - *Information it uses (inputs):* Eligible repository sessions, code, memories, and guidance (F143, F503).
   - *Output:* Updated repository memories and proposed changes to existing instruction or skill files (F143, F150, F503).
2. **Personal memory curation:**
   - *Information it uses (inputs):* Your eligible sessions and personal memories (agent conversations) (F144, F149, F503).
   - *Output:* Updated personal memories. **No changes to personal instruction or skill files** (F144, F149, F503).
3. **Code review customization:**
   - *Information it uses (inputs):* Feedback on merged pull requests and current repository code (the capability table lists pull request feedback only under this capability, not under repository customization) (F145, F151, F503).
   - *Output:* Guidance for future Copilot code reviews, turning recurring accepted code-review feedback into internal code review guidance (F145, F151, F503).

**How changes are applied and how run results are inspected.** Dreaming applies its three output types differently (F153, F154, F155, F156, F157, F470):

- **Memories:** Copilot Dreaming adds, updates, and deletes personal and repository memories **automatically, without approval for each change** (F153, F157, F470).
- **Checked-in instructions and skills:** Copilot Dreaming **proposes changes in a pull request**; checked-in files remain unchanged until a human reviews and merges the pull request, and the concept page warns that **"AI-generated guidance can be incorrect. Review proposed file changes before merging"** and notes that not every run makes changes or opens a pull request (F154, F156, F157, F470). Instructions and skills proposed in pull requests are visible to anyone with access to the repository, while Dreaming does not change who can access or manage personal memories (F158, F228, F475).
- **Code review guidance:** Copilot Dreaming **updates internal code review guidance without a pull request**, complementing the checked-in instruction files that Copilot code review also reads (F155, F470; the Dreaming concept page lists no mechanism to view or delete this internal code review guidance, F163 — see Q16).
- **Inspecting run results in the GitHub Copilot app:** Settings on GitHub.com control whether Dreaming is enabled but **do not display individual run results** (F159, F227, F494). To see what a run produced, open the **GitHub Copilot app**, select `Automations` in the sidebar, and open the `Dreaming` tab (`Personal Dream runs` or `Repository Dream runs`; repository runs appear only for repositories added to the Copilot app, whereas personal runs do not require adding a repository) (F159, F227, F494). A memory curation run shows `Memory updates` labeled `Added`, `Updated` (with `Before` and `After` text when available), or `Removed`, along with a pull request link when instruction or skill changes were proposed; however, **run details in the Copilot app are not a complete activity history and do not include an option to undo a run** (F159, F161, F228, F484, F494).

**Which repository files Dreaming can review.** Repository customization can review **eight tracked path patterns**: **`.github/copilot-instructions.md`, `.github/instructions/**`, `.github/agents/**`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `REVIEW.md`, and `.github/skills/**`** (F164, F503). It does not necessarily change every file, and which instructions, skills, and memories Copilot uses depends on the Copilot feature (F165; separately, Copilot code review reads `.github/copilot-instructions.md`, root `AGENTS.md`, `.github/instructions/**/*.instructions.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`, and `REVIEW.md`, F167). The tracked-file list contains **no `.copilot/` path** (such as `.copilot/lessons/`), and while `.github/agents/**` appears in the reviewed-files list, the page's output and change-application bullets mention only proposed instruction or skill changes without separately stating whether agent-file changes are proposed (F164, F166, F503; see Q16).

**Prerequisites: model access, plan and environment exclusions, and session syncing.** All Dreaming capabilities require an eligible paid Copilot subscription and permission under applicable enterprise and organization policies (F142, F186):

- **Model access (`GPT-6.1 Sol`) and plan exclusions:** The initial preview **requires access to `GPT-6.1 Sol` allowed by policy** across all three capabilities (`Repository customization`, `Personal memory curation`, and `Code review customization`) and **is not available on Copilot Pro** (F142, F174, F176, F183, F184, F185, F504; the Dreaming page names no other excluded paid plan and states no fallback model if `GPT-6.1 Sol` is disallowed, F174, F175 — see Q16). On GitHub's `Supported AI models in GitHub Copilot` page, `GPT-6.1 Sol` (provider OpenAI, status GA, CLI identifier `gpt-6.1-sol`) is listed as **Not included** for Copilot Pro and **Included** for **Copilot Pro+, Copilot Max, Copilot Business, and Copilot Enterprise**, with model access configurable by enterprise and organization owners for Business and Enterprise seats (F177, F178, F179, F180).
- **Data-residency and self-hosted runner exclusions:** Copilot Dreaming **does not support GitHub Enterprise Cloud with data residency** (where an enterprise is hosted on a dedicated subdomain of `GHE.com`) (F142, F181, F182, F504). In addition, **Code review customization** requires Copilot code review and its preview features to be enabled and **does not currently support self-hosted runners** (F185, F504).
- **Memory prerequisites and activation order:** *Repository customization* and *Code review customization* require repository-level Memory to be enabled; *Personal memory curation* requires Personal Memory to be enabled (F183, F184, F185, F189). Prerequisites should be completed **before** enabling Dreaming; if Dreaming is already enabled when additional prerequisites are completed, **you must turn Copilot Dreaming off and on again** using the setting that controls the affected personal memories or repositories, because completing prerequisites alone does not activate additional capabilities (F187).
- **Session syncing requirement, the `'Store local sessions in the Cloud'` policy, and `remoteExport` in `~/.copilot/settings.json`:** Session syncing is **Required** for *Repository customization* and *Personal memory curation*, and **Not required** for *Code review customization* (F147, F183, F184, F185, F505). Session syncing saves local sessions created with Copilot CLI or the GitHub Copilot app to the user's GitHub account and is **enabled by default** on the client via `"remoteExport": true` in **`~/.copilot/settings.json`** (the primary CLI configuration file in `~/.copilot`, overrideable via `COPILOT_HOME` and supporting JSON with comments; per-run overrides are `--sync-session=on` and `--sync-session=off`, and if a user previously set `"remoteExport": false`, they must remove it or set it to `true`) (F147, F190, F192, F193, F194, F195, F264, F471). For users who receive Copilot from an enterprise or organization (Copilot Business and Enterprise), the **`Store local sessions in the Cloud`** policy under `Copilot CLI` in the enterprise or organization policy settings (which is **unconfigured by default** at the organization level) **must be set to at least `View from cloud`** (syncing only; or `View and control` for syncing plus remote control); **if the policy is disabled or unconfigured, sessions remain local only** (F191, F195, F196, F505). Synced session data is tied to the user's personal account and is accessible only to that user by default (enabling session syncing does not give administrators access to a user's session data), and turning off session syncing does not delete sessions that have already been synced (F171, F197, F198, F476).

**Cost model and spending-control caveats.** Copilot Dreaming model usage is **billed in AI credits** (`1 AI credit = $0.01 USD`) as part of existing Copilot usage, not as a separate billing line item for Dreaming (F199, F201, F208, F496). Cost depends on processing and the number of enabled users and repositories, **including inactive repositories**, and GitHub Docs state explicitly: **"Every Copilot Dreaming run uses AI credits. This includes runs that make no changes or process inactive repositories. You should review repository coverage and billing controls before enabling Copilot Dreaming."** (F199, F200, F496).

- **Included AI credits, overage defaults, and unstated per-run prices:** Individual plans include 7,000 monthly AI credits on Copilot Pro+ (3,900 base + 3,100 flex) and 20,000 on Copilot Max (10,000 base + 10,000 flex), while organization/enterprise plans include pooled monthly allowances of 1,900 AI credits per user on Copilot Business and 3,900 per user on Copilot Enterprise (resetting at `00:00:00 UTC` on the first of each calendar month with no carryover of unused credits) (F208, F212). On Copilot Business and Enterprise, **additional paid usage beyond included AI credits is enabled by default** unless an administrator explicitly disables the AI credits paid usage policy in AI Controls (F209). The Dreaming page's `Costs and usage` section states no specific AI-credit or dollar amount per run, per repository, or per user (and does not mention GitHub Actions minutes), and while the `Models and pricing` page lists `GPT-6.1 Sol` token rates (`<= 272K` input tokens: `$2.00` input, `$0.10` cached input, `$2.50` cache write, `$10.00` output per 1M tokens; `> 272K` input tokens: `$4.00` input, `$0.20` cached input, `$5.00` cache write, `$15.00` output per 1M tokens), it does not state that Dreaming runs are billed at those rates (F202, F213; see Q10, Q15). Additionally, the 2026-10-28 changelog states no effectiveness or accuracy percentages for Dreaming (F152).
- **Spending-control and usage-attribution caveats:** Before expanding Dreaming across organizations, administrators must configure spending controls carefully because: (1) **budget threshold alerts alone (75%, 90%, 100%) only send email and do not stop usage** unless `Stop usage when budget limit is reached` (or `Limit usage when budget limit is reached`) is selected on spending limits (F205, F206, F210, F496); (2) while a **per-user budget** always enforces a hard stop on an individual's consumption, **a per-user budget may not cover repository-level app usage**, which is billed to the owning organization under organization-level model policies and spending controls (F205, F206, F207, F210, F496); (3) **separate budgets for individual Copilot Dreaming app installations are not available** (bundled AI credits budgets exist at whole-enterprise, single-organization, cost-center, or per-user scope, F205, F207, F211, F496); and (4) **app attribution is not available in the AI usage view** — identifying usage from a GitHub App installation requires downloading a detailed AI usage report and matching `app_client_id` to `client_id` from the `List app installations for an organization` REST API (F203, F204).

**How to enable and disable Copilot Dreaming across all four scopes, and non-reversibility.**

- **Enterprise level:** Navigate to the enterprise > `AI controls` > `Copilot` > `Features & clients` > `Configure features & clients` > `Memory` section. The Copilot Memory policy must be `Let organizations decide` or `Enabled everywhere` (`Enabling Copilot Memory does not, by itself, enable Copilot Dreaming`). Set the Dreaming policy to **`Let organizations decide`** (initially disabled for all orgs, allowing org owners to enable or disable it; recommended for a pilot), **`Enabled everywhere`** (organizations inherit an enabled policy within the Copilot Memory scope and cannot disable it at the org level, though switching later from `Enabled everywhere` to `Let organizations decide` leaves Dreaming enabled while allowing org owners to disable it), or **`Disabled everywhere`** (organizations cannot enable Dreaming) (F216, F217, F482).
- **Organization level:** Open organization `Settings` > under `Code, planning, and automation` click `Copilot` > `Policies` > `Memory` section (requires the parent enterprise policy to be `Let organizations decide`; a locked setting means the enterprise controls it, F219). **Review the `Repository access` setting first**, because enabling Dreaming enables it for **all repositories covered by the current Copilot Memory `Repository access` selection**, and removing a repository from that selection disables both Copilot Memory and Copilot Dreaming for that repository; then select `Enabled` in the `Dreaming` dropdown (F218, F224, F510).
- **Personal level (personal memories only):** Allowing Dreaming via enterprise or organization policy **does not automatically enable personal Dreaming**; each user must enable both Copilot Memory and Copilot Dreaming in their personal settings (F215, F220, F224). Open GitHub.com profile picture > `Settings` > under `Code, planning, and automation` click `Copilot` > `Features` > `Copilot Memory` (if Copilot Memory is disabled, the personal Dreaming setting is hidden) > select `Enabled` in the `Dreaming` dropdown (F221). This setting controls Dreaming for **personal memories only** and does not enable Dreaming for repositories the user owns; furthermore, **users cannot override a policy that disables Dreaming in any organization or enterprise that provides them with Copilot access, even if they also hold an individual subscription** (F215, F221, F222).
- **Repository level (personally owned repositories):** Applies only to users with an eligible individual Copilot subscription who do not receive Copilot access from an organization or enterprise and who have administrator access to the repository (**Dreaming cannot be enabled for an archived repository**, and organization-owned repositories are governed by organization/enterprise policy instead) (F223, F224). In the repository, open `Settings` > under `Code, planning, and automation` click `Copilot` > `Memory` (ensure `Allow Copilot to store and retrieve repository facts` is enabled) > click `Dreaming` in the sidebar > enable `Dreaming`, repeating for each repository (F223, F224).
- **Disabling Dreaming does not undo changes:** To stop future runs, disable Copilot Dreaming in the relevant enterprise (`Disabled everywhere`), organization, personal, or repository settings; turning off personal Dreaming does **not** stop runs for organization-owned repositories, and a run already in progress may continue (F170, F225, F484). **Turning off Copilot Dreaming does not undo its changes, delete information it has created, close its pull requests, or restore deleted memories**; existing personal memories must be reviewed and deleted in personal settings, repository memories in repository settings, and proposed instruction/skill pull requests reviewed, closed, or merged separately (F162, F169, F226, F484).

### 3.3 (C) Copilot CLI memory surface

**The `/memory` slash command and the local CLI memory setting.** In interactive Copilot CLI sessions, the local Copilot Memory setting is **on by default** (following the user's GitHub settings and applicable enterprise, organization, and repository policies), whereas in **non-interactive / programmatic CLI runs** (such as `copilot -p "YOUR PROMPT"`) Copilot Memory is **disabled by default** unless `--enable-memory` is passed for that run (F22, F28, F97, F98, F237, F238, F241, F242, F255, F295, F296):

- **Checking status (`/memory` and `/memory show`):** Added in Copilot CLI `v1.0.49` (published 2026-05-19, F266) and updated with documentation links in `v1.0.51` (2026-05-20, F268), entering `/memory` or `/memory show` in an interactive session displays the CLI's local setting and a link to manage stored memories on GitHub, with the explicit caution that an enabled local status **does not mean account or repository policies allow both types of memory** (F28, F125, F230, F231, F243).
- **Enabling and disabling (`/memory on`, `/memory off`, and `"memory": false`):** Entering `/memory off` disables **both** repository-level facts and user-level preferences in Copilot CLI; `/memory on` clears that local opt-out (F28, F98, F232, F241, F295). The choice takes effect immediately in the current session and is **saved for future interactive sessions on that machine**, independent of the user's setting on GitHub.com (changing your personal Copilot Memory setting on GitHub.com does **not** clear a CLI `/memory off` opt-out) (F28, F98, F233, F234, F241, F295). Memory can also be disabled in the CLI user configuration file (`~/.copilot/settings.json`, overrideable via `COPILOT_HOME` and supporting JSON with comments, F194, F264) by setting **`"memory": false`** (F235, F295). Neither `/memory on` nor `--enable-memory` can override applicable enterprise, organization, or repository restrictions; `--enable-memory` takes precedence over a saved `"memory": false` for a single non-interactive run (subject to account, repository, and tool-permission restrictions) without altering the saved setting (F28, F236, F239, F241, F295, F296). Turning off Copilot Memory in the CLI stops further storage and retrieval, but memories already injected into an active conversation remain in its context until the user starts a new session (F240).

**Interactive approval prompt, options, scopes, and persisted permissions.** In interactive Copilot CLI sessions (unlike Copilot cloud agent and Copilot code review, which do not prompt, F24, F245, F501), depending on the user's permission settings, Copilot can ask the user to **approve storing a memory or rating an existing memory's usefulness** (F44, F246, F288, F486):

- **What the prompt shows:** The prompt displays the memory and who can see it — whether it applies to the user (`(for user)`) or is shared with repository collaborators (`owner/repo`, `(shared with repository collaborators)`) (F44, F247, F265, F267, F288). In the Copilot SDK (`docs/features/streaming-events.md` and `nodejs/src/generated/session-events.ts` at commit `01a20449`), this corresponds to a `permission.requested` event with `kind: "memory"` (`PermissionRequestMemory`), carrying `fact`, optional `action` (`store` or `vote`), `subject`, `citations`, `scope` (`repository` or `user`), `repoNwo`, vote `direction` (`upvote` or `downvote`), `reason`, and assisted-mode-only `assistedApproval` (F270, F271).
- **The three approval options and their scopes:** At the prompt, the user can **(1) approve the update once**, **(2) reject it with optional feedback**, or **(3) choose to allow future memory updates without asking**, where the approval option states whether it applies to the **current repository, directory, or session** (F44, F248, F249, F257, F288, F486; the `create-memories` page gives no further detail on which scope is offered in which situation, F249 — see Q16).
- **Persisted tool approvals (`permissions-config.json`), CLI flags, and reset:** Choosing `"don't ask again in this repo"` (or `"in this directory"`) saves `{"kind": "memory"}` (which has no additional required or optional fields and `"Approves memory write and vote requests"`) into **`~/.copilot/permissions-config.json`** under a `locations` entry keyed by the absolute Git repository root (resolving linked worktrees to the main repository root and submodules to their own working directory) or the normalized working directory when outside a Git repository (F258, F261, F262, F263). Command-line flags `--allow-tool` and `--deny-tool` accept patterns of the form `Kind(argument)` for the `memory` kind (`"Storing facts to agent memory"`) and apply **only to the current session** without writing to `permissions-config.json` (deny rules take precedence over saved approvals), while `/reset-allowed-tools` revokes permissions granted during the current interactive session and clears all saved tool and directory approvals for the current location from `permissions-config.json` (F256, F259, F260).

**Root agent versus subagents.** In Copilot CLI, **only the root agent can call `store_memory` or `vote_memory`** to store or vote on memories (and `vote_memory` tool calls are throttled per response and per interaction since `v1.0.55` to prevent runaway voting bursts, F269). **Subagents can read/use stored memories through `read_memories`** when they have access to that tool, **but cannot write, vote on, or change the memory store** (F45, F46, F252, F253, F254, F486).

**Bulk import (`copilot memories import <file>`), flags, JSONL schema, approval semantics, and partial-failure retry.** Introduced in Copilot CLI `v1.0.85` (published 2026-09-16, F292), **`copilot memories import PATH-TO-FILE`** validates and imports prepared user-level preferences and repository-level facts in bulk from a **JSONL** file in which each line contains one versioned memory object starting `{"type":"memory"...` (F31, F272, F291; note that the CLI command reference delegates the record schema to the `create-memories` page, F291, and `manage-for-yourself` does not mention `copilot memories import` or state whether `/memory off` or `"memory": false` affects it, F294, F295, F296 — see Q16):

- **JSONL record schema (required and optional fields):**
  - **Required fields (`7`):** `type`, `version`, `scope`, `subject`, `fact`, `reason`, and `citations` (in both published example records, the first two properties are `"type":"memory"` and `"version":1`; the page states no other allowed `type` or `version` values and gives no prose definitions, length limits, or allowed-value lists for `subject`, `fact`, and `reason` beyond the examples) (F273, F275, F276, F277, F279; see Q16).
  - **Optional field (`1`):** `externalId`, used to identify a record in the import results and detect repeated IDs within the input file; **it is not stored as a permanent memory identifier** (F273, F274).
  - **User-level preference scope and citation shape:** `{"type":"memory","version":1,"externalId":"personal-testing-1","scope":{"type":"user"},"subject":"testing","fact":"Run focused tests first.","reason":"This is the user's preferred validation workflow.","citations":[{"type":"text","text":"User input: \"Run focused tests first.\""}]}` — the user scope shape is **`{"type":"user"}`** with no `repository` sub-object, paired in the example with a `text` citation object carrying a `text` string (F275, F278).
  - **Repository-level fact scope and citation shape:** `{"type":"memory","version":1,"externalId":"repo-testing-1","scope":{"type":"repository","repository":{"host":"github.com","owner":"octo-org","name":"octo-repo"}},"subject":"testing","fact":"Run npm test before opening a pull request.","reason":"The repository requires this validation workflow.","citations":[{"type":"file","path":"CONTRIBUTING.md","lineStart":10,"lineEnd":12}]}` — the repository scope shape is **`{"type":"repository","repository":{"host":"github.com","owner":"...","name":"..."}}`** (where the user must replace `octo-org` and `octo-repo` with a GitHub.com repository for which they have permission to write memories), paired in the example with a `file` citation object carrying `path`, `lineStart`, and `lineEnd` (the page does not enumerate other citation types or state which citation sub-fields are required) (F276, F278; see Q16).
- **Documented CLI flags (`--dry-run`, `--on-conflict skip`, `--output json`):**
  1. **`--dry-run`:** Validates the complete JSONL file **without authenticating or submitting any memories** (the `create-memories` two-step workflow runs `copilot memories import PATH-TO-FILE --dry-run` first and notes that it checks file format, not the account's permission to store the memories, before running `copilot memories import PATH-TO-FILE`) (F280, F281, F506).
  2. **`--on-conflict skip` (from `--on-conflict skip` or `error`, default `error`):** By default, a repeated `externalId` within the input file or an existing memory conflict reported by the service causes an error for that record; adding `--on-conflict skip` skips conflicting records instead, though **it does not guarantee that repeating an import will avoid duplicate memories** (F282, F283, F284).
  3. **`--output json`:** Returns the import result as JSON instead of human-readable text (neither the `create-memories` page nor the CLI command reference publishes a schema or example of the JSON output result, and the CLI command reference's option table for `copilot memories import` lists only these three flags) (F285, F286; see Q16).
- **Import treated as approval, and partial-failure / retry guidance:** Running `copilot memories import` is **treated as approval to submit the records**, so Copilot CLI does **not** prompt the user to approve each memory separately; normal authentication, policy, repository permission, scope validation, and secret scanning requirements still apply, and imported repository-level facts are shared with collaborators who can use Copilot Memory in that repository (F47, F250, F287, F288). **If any line fails schema validation, no records are submitted.** After schema validation succeeds, **records are submitted individually**, so a service failure can produce a **partial import**: users must inspect the per-record results and **retry only the records that failed**, because repeating the entire import can create duplicate memories (F289, F290, F506).

### 3.4 (D) SCNS — local, self-hosted memory service (alpha)

**What SCNS is, its alpha status, and canonical public URLs.** **SCNS ("Second Central Nervous System")** — canonically located at `https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md` (with issue tracker `https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/issues` and clone URL `https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns.git`, grounded in `README.md`, F301, F374) — is an **alpha**, local background service that gives a coding agent cross-session memory by watching sessions, storing facts, decisions, and observations in a local SQLite vault, isolating memory per registered repository, and injecting relevant memories at the start of prompts (F301, F303, F304, F450, F539; excluded from `specs/001-research-sota/memory.md` when private, F457). Its `README.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md`) explicitly declares **`Status: alpha. This is being handed to a small group of testers. Parts of it are solid and have been running daily for months; parts are young.`** and warns: **`"Unverified on anything but the author's machines. Native Windows support is new. The install has not been run end-to-end on a clean machine. Your install is the test."`** (F303, F312, F450, F451, F539, F556). Its `docs/NORTH_STAR.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/NORTH_STAR.md`, an architectural mission document rather than a progress tracker, F314) defines SCNS's mission as a self-improving system whose north-star measure is **"whether a human accepts an outcome or deems it a waste of time, at what cost, on real usage"**, noting that retrieval recall and static QA scores are not substitutes for that outcome (F313, F315, F569).

**Architecture: global broker (`127.0.0.1:3456`), meta-broker supervisor, per-repository child brokers (`3500–3599`), database isolation, and session-start routing.** As shown in `README.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md`) and `docs/modules.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/modules.md`), the runtime architecture consists of (F317, F452):

- **Global broker on `127.0.0.1:3456`:** A local HTTP service on `127.0.0.1:3456` that tracks live sessions, runs scheduled jobs (re-indexing, consolidation, housekeeping), keeps an event log, holds the meta-registry, and serves the memory API (F317, F318, F420, F452, F543). By default, both the global broker and every child broker bind loopback (`127.0.0.1`), which avoids triggering a Windows Defender Firewall prompt and is overridden only via `BROKER_BIND` (e.g., `0.0.0.0`) for deliberate LAN exposure (F318, F319, F432, F543, F544).
