Setting `BROKER_TOKEN` (unset by default = auth off) turns on `Authorization: Bearer <token>` enforcement for every HTTP route **except two deliberate exemptions**: `/health*` (so liveness probes need no secret) and `/api/hooks/*` (because Copilot lifecycle hooks fire before a token can be threaded and carry observational session events over loopback under the default bind) (F320, F393, F432, F544; see F393 caveat).
- **Meta-broker supervisor and per-repository child brokers on ports `3500–3599`:** Registering a repository (`meta-broker register <path>`) tells the meta-broker supervisor to spawn and supervise a dedicated child broker for that repository on a port from the **`3500–3599` pool, with its own separate database** (verifiable via `node dist/cli.js meta-broker list` or `http://127.0.0.1:3456/api/meta/resolve?path=<repo-root>`) (F310, F317, F321, F324, F330, F334, F444, F452, F552). **Internal documentation inconsistency on how the meta-broker supervisor runs (`see Q14(e)`):** the `README.md` architecture diagram draws the `"meta-broker supervisor"` inside the `"global broker :3456"` box and the `#Per-repository child brokers` section describes it as `"a loop inside the global broker"` (F317, F321), whereas the `#Register your repositories` and `#Start it automatically at logon` sections instruct the user to run a separate foreground command (`node dist\cli.js meta-broker run`) that has no HTTP listener of its own, tracks liveness via `~/.scns/run/meta-broker.pid`, and is supervised alongside the global broker by the logon watchdog when projects exist in the registry (F324, F325, F444; see Q14).
- **Structural per-repository isolation and once-per-session route resolution:** When a Copilot CLI session starts, the extension asks the global broker `"which broker owns this directory?"` and talks directly to that child broker from then on; two repositories cannot see each other's memory because they use **separate databases behind separate ports** rather than relying on a prompt asking the model to be careful (F308, F322, F396, F552, F553; verified from SCNS documentation rather than independent testing, F308, F553 caveats). **The route is resolved once, at session start**, and cached for the life of the session (re-resolving once only if a later request fails, and automatic child recovery preserves existing port anchors so pinned CLI clients are not stranded; issue `#48` fixed a defect where a single transient probe failure silently latched a session to the global brain) (F323, F324, F329, F331, F332, F395, F444, F552, F556). Consequently, registering a repository or setting an environment variable mid-session has **no effect until a new session is started** (F323, F324, F327, F329, F395, F435, F444, F554).
- **Failure mode of a dead meta-broker and the strict-routing guard (`SCNS_REQUIRE_PROJECT_BROKER=1`):** The `README.md` warns that **"a dead meta-broker fails quietly and completely: every per-project child dies with it, and every session in every registered repo silently resolves to `global:fallback` and writes to the shared brain instead of its own project — while the global broker stays perfectly healthy and every other check passes"** (`scns doctor` checks this as `meta-broker-supervision`, and a correctly routed session logs `SCNS extension loaded (broker: http://127.0.0.1:35NN [project:<id>])` rather than `global:default` or `global:fallback`) (F326, F328, F435, F436, F555). Setting **`SCNS_REQUIRE_PROJECT_BROKER=1`** (read when the Copilot CLI process starts, classified under `Solid` in the README) makes SCNS **refuse to write memory when a session did not resolve to a per-project child broker**, guarding against cross-repo pollution of the shared global brain (and misrouted historical data is inspected and repaired via `audit-cross-project-pollution.mjs` and `repair-cross-project-pollution.mjs` using a reviewed JSON plan and `--apply --backup-dir <new-absolute-directory>`) (F310, F327, F329, F337, F435, F554, F556, F557).

**The memory vault (`~/.scns/memory/vault.db`, FTS5, `sqlite-vec` 384-dim local embeddings, and graph layer).** In `docs/modules.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/modules.md`), `resolveScnsHome()` returns `SCNS_HOME` when set or `os.homedir()` otherwise, deriving every `~/.scns/...` path (`vault.db`, `experience.db`, `dream.db`, telemetry, state, secrets) from it (F317, F338, F438). The `src/memory/` module (`README.md` and `docs/modules.md`) combines a file-based markdown vault (`SOUL.md` for core identity, `USER.md` for profile, `MEMORY.md` for consolidated facts, and daily logs for raw observations, using atomic writes and path-traversal prevention in `vault.ts`) with a SQLite database at **`~/.scns/memory/vault.db`** using two SQLite extensions plus a graph layer (F310, F339, F347, F421, F540, F564):

- **FTS5 keyword search:** Synchronous lexical indexing (`chunks_fts`), so a stored fact is findable immediately via `scns_memory_search` the moment it is stored (F340, F342, F346, F348, F397, F540).
- **`sqlite-vec` 384-dimensional local embeddings:** Semantic search (`chunk_embeddings`) using **384-dimensional embeddings** computed locally via FastEmbed ONNX (`BGE-small` in `embeddings.ts`, lazy-loaded on first use) and fused with FTS5 at query time (`0.7×vector + 0.3×keyword` in `search.ts`) (F341, F342, F344, F348, F425, F540). While the vault section calls the ~128 MB embedding model `"a bundled model"` (`No text leaves your machine`), the Windows install and troubleshooting sections clarify that **the first embedding on a fresh install downloads ~128 MB into `local_cache/`** (which can cause injected context to lag until the download finishes) unless the user copies an existing `local_cache/` folder into the repo root or sets `SCNS_EMBED_CACHE_DIR` (F344, F345, F346, F397, F425, F426, F540, F565; see F344 and F426 caveats).
- **Graph layer (alpha / young):** `vault.db` also includes graph tables (`memory_entities`, `memory_edges`, `memory_causal`) for multi-hop retrieval following chains of related facts rather than returning only top-$k$ chunks; `README.md` explicitly marks the graph/causal layer as **"the newest and least proven part"** and lists it under **"Young — works, but expect rough edges"** (F311, F343, F413, F540, F570).

**MAGMA-style retrieval (`Anchor` → `Traverse` → `Synthesize`; `3,400` estimated-token default cap).** Grounded in `README.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md`), `docs/PRIOR_ART.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/PRIOR_ART.md`), and `docs/modules.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/modules.md`), SCNS's retrieval architecture was written against the paper *MAGMA: A Multi-Graph based Agentic Memory Architecture for AI Agents* (Dongming Jiang, Yi Li, Guanpeng Li, Bingzhe Li, arXiv:2601.03236, January 2026; reference implementation consulted 2026-07-06, retaining parameter values relative drop `θ = 0.15`, max depth `5`, and max nodes budget `200`, while noting in `§M.2` that SCNS's consolidation gate previously starved causal candidates and needed semantic edges `E_sem`) (F352, F359, F360, F364, F365, F366). On the default live briefing path (setting `SCNS_LIVE_MEMORY_BRIEFING=hybrid` falls back to the hybrid join without a rebuild, though the README notes neither default was re-confirmed on a fresh install, F356, F562), SCNS executes three steps (F310, F352):

1. **Anchor:** A hybrid FTS5 and vector search selects the entry-point chunks (`SearchResult.isAnchor`), which are protected as an unconditional floor (`AnchorRetention=1.0`) so nodes reached on later hops never evict them (F353, F354, F362, F363).
2. **Traverse:** `src/memory/adaptive-traversal.ts` walks outward from those anchors along typed graph edges, scoring each hop by question-conditioned salience on the edge type (literal MAGMA Eq-5 log-salience `λ1·φ + λ2·sim`) rather than raw cosine similarity, so a `caused-by` edge can outrank a chunk that merely sounds similar (F354, F363, F367).
3. **Synthesize:** The entire visited subgraph flows to Stage-4 narrative synthesis and is ordered and serialized into a single `Relevant SCNS memories:` block capped in **tokens rather than chunks** via **`SCNS_MAGMA_MAX_TOKENS` (`3,400` estimated tokens by default)**, eliding the low-salience non-anchor tail instead of cutting context mid-thought (F355, F356, F361, F362, F562).

**Alpha caveats on MAGMA retrieval and query routing:** SCNS leaves MAGMA's intent-routing lever off on the live path (`intentRouting: false`) and treats `magma` (single-hop) and `multihop-iter` (iterative multi-hop chaining) as purpose-routed arms rather than rivals; however, **no live query classifier exists yet** (`src/memory/hop-shape.ts` runs in shadow/offline mode only while the live arms are sampled to collect balanced evidence, and retrieval levers such as `SCNS_NARRATIVE_SYNTHESIS=1`, `SCNS_MULTIHOP_ITER=1`, and `SCNS_EDGE_WEIGHTS=1` in `docs/modules.md` follow a default-off measure-before-flip convention) (F311, F316, F357, F367, F368, F413, F415, F417). On the LoCoMo benchmark at the ~3.4k-token budget, the README self-reports an overall score of `0.768` against a `0.700` target, but records strict paper reproduction as **`NOT_REPRODUCED`** because temporal questions land below the paper's per-category result and **a scrambled-graph control suggested that graph topology is not carrying the result on static QA** (so Stage-4 ordering and token budgeting may be doing the work on its own) (F311, F358, F423, F568; see F358, F423, F568 caveats and Q15).

**The Copilot CLI extension (`21` tools, context injection off by default, and session hooks).** Grounded in `README.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md`), `docs/copilot-integration.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/copilot-integration.md`), and `src/copilot-extension/tool-definitions.ts` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/src/copilot-extension/tool-definitions.ts`), SCNS integrates with Copilot CLI as a single generated file at **`~/.copilot/extensions/scns/extension.mjs`** (F310, F317, F377, F558; note that GitHub's own documentation states that Copilot CLI extensions are an experimental feature subject to change and execute on the user's computer with the user's privileges, F573):

- **The `21` extension tools:** `TOOL_DEFINITIONS` in `src/copilot-extension/tool-definitions.ts` is the source of truth and defines **21 tools** across five functional groups — **6 memory and status tools** (`scns_memory_search` requiring `query` with optional `top_k` defaulting to `5`; `scns_memory_store` requiring `key` and `value` with no scope, type, or repository parameter; `scns_memory_delete`; `scns_suggestions`; `scns_status`; and `scns_report_negative_feedback` requiring `feedback` with optional `context`, `category`, `target`, and `severity`) and **15 multi-agent messaging, team/file-intent coordination, shared-state, and task tools** (`scns_send_message`, `scns_read_messages`, `scns_ack_message`, `scns_report_progress`, `scns_heartbeat`, `scns_team_status`, `scns_list_teams`, `scns_declare_intent`, `scns_release_intent`, `scns_list_intents`, `scns_set_state`, `scns_get_state`, `scns_create_task`, `scns_list_tasks`, `scns_update_task`) (F375, F376, F377, F379, F380, F558, F559). In `createToolHandlers`, each tool calls the SCNS broker over HTTP with a **`500ms` timeout (`timeoutMs = 500`) and never throws**, returning JSON with `available: false` and `'HTTP <status>'` or `'SCNS broker unavailable'` on failure (F378, F559).
- **Context injection is off by default and how it is turned on:** When enabled, the extension's `onUserPromptSubmitted` hook queries the broker for relevant memories and suggestions and prepends them as hidden context (`Relevant SCNS memories:`) that the agent sees and the user does not; without it, searching memory must be invoked explicitly via tool calls (F356, F371, F381, F382, F558, F562). Context injection is **off by default** (neither `README.md` nor `docs/copilot-integration.md` states a rationale for why off was chosen as the default, F385, F386) and can be turned on in two ways (F383):
  1. **Install-time flag (recommended by the README):** Generate the extension with `scns install-extension --context-injection` (`node dist/cli.js install-extension --context-injection`), which **hard-wires injection ON in `extension.mjs` so the broker's `extension.config` state is never consulted** (F383, F385, F445; note that while the README overview says injection is `"off by default and takes two switches to turn on"`, Trap 3 in the same README states that `--context-injection` alone is sufficient and skips the broker switch entirely, F371, F385, F558 — see Q14).
  2. **Broker-state toggle (when generated without `--context-injection`):** POST `extension.config` with `{"contextInjectionEnabled": true}` to `POST /api/state` (cached for 60 seconds; example `GET /api/extension/config` response is `{contextInjectionEnabled: false, maxMemories: 5, maxSuggestions: 3}`) (F383, F384, F563). Because broker state is **per-broker**, setting `extension.config` only on the global broker at `:3456` does nothing inside a registered repository whose session talks to a child broker on `:3500+` — it must be set on the child broker's port as well (F336, F384).
- **Forwarding of session hooks, shell-hook alternative, and passive poller:** The extension forwards all **6 SDK session hooks** (`session start`, `session end`, `user prompt`, `pre-tool-use`, `post-tool-use`, `error`) to the broker as session events, and `install-extension` also bakes session-start broker health reconciliation into the extension (`SCNS_SESSION_RECONCILE=0` disables it) (F377, F388, F394, F558). Note that `docs/copilot-integration.md` Layer 1 states `"The HTTP timeout is 5000 ms by default"` (configurable via `install-extension --timeout <ms>`, `TIMEOUT_MS`) for event forwarding, whereas its Graceful Degradation section states `"All broker communication uses 500ms timeouts with fire-and-forget semantics"` without reconciling the two numbers (F388, F391, F566; see Q14). When the broker is down or memory is unavailable, the extension degrades gracefully by skipping event forwarding and returning `{additionalContext: null}` (F392, F566). For environments without extensions (such as CI/CD or cloud agents), `scns setup-hooks` generates curl-based shell hooks in `hooks.json` mapping the six Copilot hook events (`Use either the extension OR hooks, not both — they would produce duplicate events`), and Layer 2 provides an optional passive poller (`OBSERVE_SESSION_STORE_ENABLED=true`, default interval `60000 ms`, max sessions `50`) that reads `~/.copilot/session-store.db` (F389, F390, F560).

**Memory pruning, retire, and restore commands and their safety posture.** Grounded in `README.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md`), `docs/copilot-integration.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/copilot-integration.md`), and `src/copilot-extension/tool-definitions.ts` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/src/copilot-extension/tool-definitions.ts`), SCNS provides three manual, directory-scoped CLI commands (`scns memory prune --directory <dir>`, `scns memory delete <path> --directory <dir>`, and `scns memory restore <path> --directory <dir>`) plus the `scns_memory_delete` extension tool (`scns_memory_delete` is the only removal tool among the 21 extension tools; `prune` and `restore` are CLI-only, F410) (F333, F398, F399, F400, F549, F551):

- **Deepest-registered-root resolution and refusal on unregistered/global routes:** Every removal command resolves the owning project through the meta-registry (the deepest registered root containing `--directory`) so it operates only on that project's vault — never the home vault and never a parent's — and **refuses** any directory outside a registered project (or a `scns_memory_delete` call on a global route) rather than silently redirecting (F333, F398, F409, F549). If the project's child broker is running, that process owns the vault and the CLI refuses to open a second writer, while an exclusive lease on the vault directory serializes deletions against re-indexing across processes (F408).
- **Preview-by-default, retire-by-default, and 4-representation reconciliation:** Both `scns memory delete <path> --directory <dir>` and `scns_memory_delete` **preview by default** and require `--apply` / `apply=true` to execute (F399, F400, F409, F551). The default removal mode is **`retire`**, which copies the exact bytes into the vault's `archive/` tier (excluded from listing and indexing, and recorded so a later re-index cannot resurrect the removed revision) and can be undone with `scns memory restore <path> --directory <dir>` (which revives the record and re-projects its instruction bullet on the next application pass); passing `--hard` / `hard=true` permanently deletes with no recovery record (F399, F400, F401, F402, F550, F551). Because removing a Markdown file alone leaves stale index state behind, deletion reconciles **all four representations** of a memory together: (1) `vault_files` / `chunks` / `chunks_fts` rows, (2) vectors in `chunk_embeddings` (a `vec0` table with no foreign-key cascade), (3) graph edges, and (4) the managed bullet inside `.github/copilot-instructions.md` (reporting, never creating, an absent `.github/copilot-instructions.md`) (F349, F350, F400, F403, F542, F551).
- **Evidence-gated `scns memory prune`:** `scns memory prune --directory <dir>` is read-only by default, can save a reviewable plan (`--out prune-plan.json`) and execute that exact plan (`--apply prune-plan.json`), **proposes rather than deciding alone**, and keeps **pinned memories** outright (`scns_memory_delete` also protects pinned memories unless `allow_pinned=true` is passed, though the README's pruning section documents no command or flag for pinning a memory, F400, F404, F405, F549, F551 — see Q16). Every removal proposal in `prune` must cite evidence present in the work snapshot (a closed task, live session, unread message, or real human turn) and requires an isolated deny-all, think-only ACP evaluator session (`--evaluator-cwd`); without `--evaluator-cwd` or without verified snapshot citations, proposals are downgraded to **`uncertain`**, and `uncertain` is never treated as `keep` (F406, F407, F549, F575). (Separately, `docs/modules.md` documents `src/dream/` as an event-driven `AutoDream` consolidation daemon using `orient→gather→consolidate→prune` in a separate SQLite database without mentioning preview or restore steps, while the README roadmap lists dream/consolidation passes under `"Speculative — ideas, not commitments"`, F372, F373, F418, F419, F571, F572 — see Q7, Q16.)

**The learning loop (`experience.db`) and its honest alpha maturity caveats.** Grounded in `README.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md`), `docs/NORTH_STAR.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/NORTH_STAR.md`), and `docs/modules.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/docs/modules.md`), every memory briefing writes a `retrieval_experience` row to **`~/.scns/memory/experience.db`** recording the query, the retrieval arm that served it, and the selection propensity; a later interaction signal settles the outcome — re-asking the same question labels the earlier briefing **`waste`**, while answering a clarifying question SCNS asked labels it **`accept`** (`Accept-vs-waste is the project's north-star metric`, Strategic Priority 3 in `NORTH_STAR.md`) (F313, F351, F411, F422, F541, F569). However, the `README.md` and `docs/modules.md` state blunt **alpha maturity caveats** that any teaching material must carry (F304, F311, F412, F567):

- **"This is the least finished part of SCNS."** The learning loop, the graph/causal layer, query routing between `magma` and `multihop-iter`, suggestions and the autonomy ladder, the self-improvement loop (off by default), and the dashboard are all classified under **"Young — works, but expect rough edges"** (F304, F311, F412, F413, F414, F567, F570).
- **Contaminated early labels and deleted case-based recall:** Label quality is unproven; early `accept` labels were contaminated by non-human traffic (a sub-agent's own prompt and an automated idle notice both settled as though a human had answered) (F311, F412, F567). Furthermore, `docs/modules.md` records that the `"been-here-before"` case-based recall module (`src/memory/similar-episode.ts`) **was deleted on 2026-08-02 (`DP-F5`)** because its `(intent, target)` key was unmatchable (`target` was `NULL` on all `2,060` live `retrieval_experience` rows), concluding that `"Reuse/avoid advice is therefore not implemented today"` (F416).
- **No learned weight feeds back into live retrieval today:** In `docs/modules.md`, all learning levers (`SCNS_EDGE_WEIGHTS=1`, `SCNS_EXPLORE_ROUTING=1`) are **DEFAULT-OFF**, the hop-shape classifier runs **SHADOW/OFFLINE only**, the weekly review no longer computes an arm-promotion verdict, and the reward learner is parked (F367, F368, F415, F417). As `README.md` summarizes: **"No learned weight feeds back into live retrieval so far. Treat it as instrumentation being made trustworthy, not as a system that is learning from you today."** (F369, F370, F412, F567).

**Safety defaults, what SCNS is NOT, local-only data, and platform/install requirements.**

- **Safety defaults (daemons that push, open PRs, file issues, or spend credits are off by default):** `README.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md`) documents five background daemons that can act outside the machine or consume Copilot credits — `gate-pass-publisher` (`BROKER_PUBLISH_GATE_PASS=1`, pushes `scns/auto/*` branches and opens PRs via `gh`), `north-star-nudge` (`BROKER_NORTH_STAR_NUDGE=1`, files a GitHub issue when project intent goes stale), `drift-watchdog` (`SCNS_DRIFT_WATCHDOG=1`, files/refreshes a GitHub staleness issue), `team-task-autospawner` (`BROKER_AUTOSPAWN_ENABLED=1`, spawns agent sessions that consume credits), and `self-improve-followups` (`BROKER_SELF_IMPROVE_FOLLOWUPS=1`, queues follow-up tasks for the autospawner) (F427, F428, F546). **All five are off by default**; `SCNS_UNATTENDED=1` enables the whole family at once, an explicit per-feature `=0` always wins over `SCNS_UNATTENDED=1`, any unrecognized value is treated as off, and `scns doctor` reports the `unattended-actions` posture directly (F427, F429, F430, F448, F546; see Q14 on `[info]` vs `[WARN]`). Similarly, all optional observers (`file changes, git activity, terminal history, session hooks`) **default to off** and pass captured text through a secret sanitizer before storage (F431, F547); `consult_source` egress is gated by `checkEgress` with an **empty, human-owned `config/safety-allowlist.json`** (`safety guardrails remain human-owned`, F434, F548); and progressive autonomy levels (`L0 Observer` → `L1 Advisor` → `L2 Assistant` → `L3 Autonomous`) are persisted in `~/.scns/state/autonomy.json` (F437; default level unstated — see Q16).
- **What SCNS is NOT:** `README.md` (`https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md`) states three explicit non-goals that anchor Lab 10's teaching (F305, F306, F307, F387, F561):
  1. **Not a replacement for good instructions files:** `AGENTS.md` and `copilot-instructions.md` are still how you set the rules; SCNS is how the agent remembers what happened (F305, F387, F561).
  2. **Not a hosted product:** It is a local service you run yourself, and it will occasionally need a restart (F306, F387, F561).
  3. **Not magic retrieval:** If you never store anything and never enable context injection, SCNS does approximately nothing (F307, F387, F561).
- **Local-only data (nothing uploaded):** Everything runs on the user's own machine, the memory vault is a local SQLite file under `~/.scns/` (`SCNS_HOME`), embeddings are computed locally (after the initial ~128 MB model download into `local_cache/` unless pre-seeded), and **nothing is uploaded anywhere** (F302, F309, F344, F345, F424, F425, F426, F539, F540, F553, F565).
- **Platform and install requirements (linkable summary for Lab 10):** Lab 10 links to `https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md` rather than running the install (`specs/labs/lab10.md` non-goal). The documented Windows path targets **Windows 10/11 with PowerShell and no WSL required** (macOS `launchd` and Linux `systemd` are also documented, with `~/.scns/.internal-token` and secret vault permissions set to `0600` on POSIX and relying on default `%USERPROFILE%` NTFS ACLs on Windows, where `install-extension` embeds `BROKER_TOKEN` into `~/.copilot/extensions/scns/extension.mjs` with default file permissions suitable for a single-user machine, not a shared one) (F433, F439, F446, F447, F544, F545; see Q14 on `node dist/cli.js install` Windows support wording). Prerequisites are: (1) a supported **Node.js major version — Node `20, 22, 23, 24, or 25`** matching the `win32-x64` native ABI prebuilds (`115, 127, 131, 137, 141`) of `better-sqlite3@~12.9` (**Node `21` and anything newer than `25` have no prebuild, are unsupported, and fail fast in `doctor`**), and (2) **Git** (with `gh` optional and needed only for default-off GitHub-writing daemons) (F440, F441). Installation clones `https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns.git`, runs `npm ci` (fetching native prebuilds for `better-sqlite3`, `sqlite-vec`, and `onnxruntime-node` via `fastembed`; set `HTTPS_PROXY` behind a corporate proxy) and `npm run build`, invokes commands via **`node dist/cli.js <subcommand>`** (because `scns` is not placed on `PATH` unless the user runs `npm link`), starts the broker (`node dist/cli.js start`), registers repos (`meta-broker register <path>`) and runs the supervisor (described as a loop inside the global broker in F317 and F321, and as the foreground `meta-broker run` command or the logon `Startup\scns-watchdog.cmd` shim in F324, F325, F444, and F446; see Q14), generates the extension (`install-extension --context-injection`), and verifies health with the 15-check `doctor` command and a two-repository `scns_memory_store` / `scns_memory_search` isolation round-trip (F317, F321, F324, F325, F335, F345, F374, F442, F443, F444, F445, F446, F448, F449, F565).

### 3.5 (E) Evidence-backed trade-offs across the memory spectrum

Using **only** the verified claims in `§3` (and pointing to `§4` wherever primary sources are silent or conflicted), the three Layer 2 recall options taught in Lab 10 — **(1) Native GitHub Copilot Memory + Copilot Dreaming**, **(2) the in-repo Markdown `'lessons'` wiki (`.copilot/lessons/` + `~/.copilot/lessons/` + `/consolidate-lessons`)**, and **(3) a custom local memory service (`SCNS`, alpha)** — compare across the nine required dimensions as follows:

- **1. Where data lives:**
  - *Native Copilot Memory + Dreaming:* Stored in **the cloud** (`to store repository facts and user preferences in the cloud`; `Repository memories require a GitHub-hosted repository`, F469; see F469 caveat). Repository-level facts belong to one specific repository and are viewed and deleted in repository settings (`Settings` > `Copilot` > `Memory`), while user-level preferences are stored in the user's personal settings (`https://github.com/settings/copilot/memory`) and — on Business and Enterprise plans — owned by the active billing entity (F12, F13, F14, F111, F468, F508). When Dreaming is enabled, it uses previously synced sessions (`Store local sessions in the Cloud` / `remoteExport`), repository content, memories, and PR review feedback, and applies changes to memories, pull requests proposing changes to checked-in instructions and skills, and internal code review guidance (F143, F144, F145, F153, F154, F155, F470, F471; the concept page states no storage region or encryption-at-rest detail, F512 — see Q16).
  - *In-repo Markdown lessons wiki:* Plain Markdown files (`index.md` catalog, append-only `log.md` journal, and one `<slug>.md` file per durable lesson with a revision log) stored in **`.copilot/lessons/`** inside the git repository (project scope, committed and shipped with the repo) and **`~/.copilot/lessons/`** on the developer's workstation (global scope, personal and never committed) (F513, F514, F515, F526, F536).
  - *SCNS (alpha, `https://github.com/johnhain-msft/scns/blob/main/README.md`):* Stored **exclusively on the local machine** under `~/.scns/` (`SCNS_HOME`), including the SQLite vault at **`~/.scns/memory/vault.db`** (backing markdown files `SOUL.md`, `USER.md`, `MEMORY.md`, and daily logs, FTS5 tables, `sqlite-vec` 384-dim embeddings, and graph tables), per-repository child broker databases, `~/.scns/memory/experience.db`, `dream.db`, and a managed bullet block inside `.github/copilot-instructions.md`; nothing is uploaded anywhere (F302, F317, F338, F339, F347, F349, F351, F424, F438, F539, F540, F541, F542, F564).

- **2. Who can see it:**
  - *Native Copilot Memory + Dreaming:* Repository-level facts can be used by anyone with read access to the repository who can use Copilot Memory, and viewed in repository settings by users with **write access and user-level preferences enabled** (F6, F127, F128, F472; vs VS Code doc wording at F132, F474 — see Q13). User-level preferences can be seen by the user, by anyone who can view a shared agent session in which the preference appears, and — on Copilot Business and Enterprise — by **organization and enterprise administrators**, who can view and export preferences owned by their billing entity (F7, F13, F15, F111, F114, F115, F473, F481). Synced session data read by Dreaming is accessible only to the user by default (enabling session syncing does not grant administrators access to session transcripts), while instruction and skill changes proposed by Dreaming in pull requests are visible to anyone with repository access (F158, F197, F475, F476).
  - *In-repo Markdown lessons wiki:* Project lessons in `.copilot/lessons/` are committed to git and visible to anyone with read access to the repository; personal lessons in `~/.copilot/lessons/` are visible only on the user's local workstation and are never committed (F514, F515).
  - *SCNS (alpha):* Local to the user's workstation. Brokers bind `127.0.0.1` by default; optional `BROKER_TOKEN` enforces bearer authentication on all HTTP routes except `/health*` and `/api/hooks/*`; and token files use `0600` permissions on macOS/Linux or default `%USERPROFILE%` NTFS ACLs on Windows, with the token embedded in `~/.copilot/extensions/scns/extension.mjs` documented as acceptable for a single-user machine, **not a shared machine** (F302, F318, F319, F320, F393, F432, F433, F447, F543, F544, F545).

- **3. Governance and administrator control:**
  - *Native Copilot Memory + Dreaming:* Provides centralized **enterprise, organization, and repository administration** on GitHub.com: separate policies for user-level preferences (`Let organizations decide`, `Enabled everywhere`, `Disabled everywhere`, most-restrictive wins across multi-org licenses) and repository memory access (`All repositories` default, `Selected repositories`, `No repositories`), repository-level admin switch (`Allow Copilot to store and retrieve repository facts`), configurable repository-fact retention (`7–180` days, shorter wins between org and enterprise), Dreaming policies, AI model access policies (`GPT-6.1 Sol`), cloud session-sync policies (`Store local sessions in the Cloud`), billing-entity ownership, bulk/per-user JSONL export (`10/hour` limit, `2-hour` reuse) and permanent deletion, audit-log events (`action:copilot.memory`, `action:org.copilot_memory`, `action:business.copilot_memory`, `action:copilot.memory_created` without logging content or citations), system-level secret and model-control-token rejection, CLI root-agent-only write/vote enforcement, and interactive CLI/app approval prompts (F45, F61, F63, F64, F73, F74, F82, F91, F92, F93, F94, F96, F115, F116, F117, F122, F129, F180, F191, F216, F218, F477, F478, F479, F480, F481, F482, F485, F486, F487). Conversely, Dreaming runs on a schedule managed solely by GitHub that cannot be manually triggered or customized, applies memory and internal code review guidance changes without per-change approval, and turning Dreaming off does not undo its changes (F153, F155, F160, F169, F470, F483, F484).
  - *In-repo Markdown lessons wiki:* Governed through **standard git and pull-request review** (matching Microsoft's guidance to put reviewed team conventions in source-controlled docs or custom instructions, F538, and GitHub's head-branch evaluation of instruction/skill changes in PRs, F535) plus the human-invoked **`/consolidate-lessons`** command skill (`disable-model-invocation: true`, `--dry-run` diff preview, surgical non-consolidation edits, explicit `CONTRADICTION` reporting that leaves conflicting files untouched for a human reviewer unless marked `scope: project-override`, individual file staging without auto-committing, and no shipped cron) (F516, F517, F518, F519, F520). Secret protection is a written authoring rule in `lessons.instructions.md` (`No secrets`), not an automated scanner in the wiki mechanism itself (F521).
  - *SCNS (alpha):* Governance in the verified SCNS sources is local and operator-controlled (`safety guardrails remain human-owned`, F548): all five outward-acting or credit-spending daemons (`SCNS_UNATTENDED`) and all observers default to **off**, observer captures run through a secret sanitizer, `consult_source` egress uses an empty human-owned allowlist (`config/safety-allowlist.json`), `scns memory prune` and `scns_memory_delete` default to **read-only preview** and **recoverable `retire`** (requiring snapshot evidence citations and `--evaluator-cwd` for `prune`), and `SCNS_REQUIRE_PROJECT_BROKER=1` blocks memory writes when a session is not routed to a per-project child broker (F327, F399, F400, F401, F404, F406, F407, F427, F428, F429, F431, F434, F546, F547, F548, F549, F550, F551, F554).

- **4. Determinism (advisory vs binding):**
  - *Native Copilot Memory + Dreaming:* Explicitly **advisory and non-deterministic**. Prompting Copilot to store a memory does not guarantee it will be stored, stored memories are not guaranteed to be sent or applied in every session (upvoted memories are more likely to be sent), and GitHub Docs state that a stored fact **should not be relied on as a rule that will always be followed**, directing users to custom instructions for consistent rules (while noting that custom instructions themselves do not guarantee deterministic AI compliance every time) (F49, F51, F52, F53, F57, F488, F489, F490, F491).
  - *In-repo Markdown lessons wiki:* Explicitly teaches the boundary **`"Lessons are advisory; instructions are binding"`** (in the repository's authoring policy): patterns that should surface only when relevant stay in Layer 2 (`.copilot/lessons/`, where recall in Lab 10 relies on prompting the agent to check `.copilot/lessons/index.md` and `log.md` because `.copilot/lessons/` is not in Copilot CLI's automatic instruction-discovery paths), whereas rules that must apply on every task are promoted during `/consolidate-lessons` into Layer 3 (`AGENTS.md` or `.github/instructions/*.instructions.md`, where `applyTo` globs govern attachment and edits require starting or resuming a CLI session) (F491, F522, F523, F524, F525, F533).
  - *SCNS (alpha):* Explicitly **advisory and not a replacement for instruction files** (`"AGENTS.md and copilot-instructions.md are still how you set the rules; SCNS is how the agent remembers what happened"`). If nothing is stored and context injection is not enabled, SCNS does approximately nothing; when context injection is enabled, retrieval is bounded by a token budget (`SCNS_MAGMA_MAX_TOKENS`, `3,400` estimated tokens default) and prepended as hidden context (F305, F307, F355, F356, F382, F387, F558, F561, F562).

- **5. Inspectability and diff-ability:**
  - *Native Copilot Memory + Dreaming:* **Partially inspectable** through GitHub.com settings (or Business/Enterprise admin JSONL exports). Settings pages show memory text, subject area, agent, model, and billing entity, but **do not display citations, reason for storing, the repository-fact-vs-user-preference indicator, vote counts/voting details, or expiry datetime**, and **hide memories that Copilot has learned not to use** even while they remain stored until expiry (F16, F58, F59, F80, F81, F492). Users cannot create or edit individual memories in GitHub.com settings (F31, F130, F493); audit logs never include memory content, citations, or reasons (F82, F480); Dreaming's proposed instruction/skill changes arrive as reviewable pull requests, but its memory updates happen automatically (viewable as `Added`/`Updated`/`Removed` in the Copilot app's `Automations` > `Dreaming` tab, which is not a complete activity history and offers no undo button) and its internal code review guidance has no view/delete mechanism described on the Dreaming page (F153, F154, F155, F159, F161, F163, F470, F484, F494; see Q16).
  - *In-repo Markdown lessons wiki:* **Fully inspectable and diff-able in git** (`"a file you can cat, diff, revert, and commit"`), with an append-only `log.md` history, per-topic revision logs, `--dry-run` diff output from `/consolidate-lessons`, and a documented three-command inspection pattern (`git status`, `git diff`, and `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard .copilot/lessons/` so newly created untracked topic files are not missed by `git diff --stat`) (F513, F515, F516, F526, F527, F528).
  - *SCNS (alpha):* **Locally inspectable at the storage layer, opaque at the live prompt-injection layer.** The SQLite databases (`~/.scns/memory/vault.db`, `experience.db`), markdown vault files (`SOUL.md`, `USER.md`, `MEMORY.md`, daily logs), and managed `.github/copilot-instructions.md` block can be opened, queried directly, and reconciled via `scns memory prune`/`delete` preview plans; however, live context injection prepends a hidden `Relevant SCNS memories:` block that **"the agent sees but you don't"**, and `"Better visibility into what SCNS decided to inject, and why it picked that"` remains an unbuilt roadmap item under `Next` (F309, F347, F349, F350, F356, F370, F371, F382, F403, F542, F553, F558, F562, F564, F571).

- **6. Cost model:**
  - *Native Copilot Memory + Dreaming:* Copilot CLI, Copilot Chat, and Copilot cloud agent consume **AI credits** (`1 AI credit = $0.01 USD`) based on model and tokens used, and memories included in model context count toward token usage and AI credits (F68, F208, F495, F497). **Copilot Dreaming consumes AI credits on every background run — including runs that make no changes or process inactive repositories** — billed as part of existing Copilot usage with no published per-run credit/dollar figure, no per-installation budget cap, and per-user budgets potentially not covering organization-level repository app runs (F199, F200, F202, F205, F207, F213, F496; see Q10, Q15).
  - *In-repo Markdown lessons wiki:* **Zero background server or scheduled daemon cost** (`no cron is shipped in this repo`, F520). Reading and consolidating markdown files during interactive or command-driven Copilot CLI sessions consumes normal session tokens/AI credits (`labs/lab10.md` and `.copilot/lessons/README.md` describe this as `~15k in / ~5k out` and `"costs pennies"`, which are unmeasured prose estimates rather than reproducible benchmarks, F497, F529, F530, F531 — see Q15).
  - *SCNS (alpha):* Running the local broker, SQLite vault, FTS5 index, and local ONNX `BGE-small` embeddings incurs **zero cloud API or AI-credit cost** (F344, F348, F540). When context injection is enabled, the prepended briefing adds up to `SCNS_MAGMA_MAX_TOKENS` (**`3,400` estimated tokens by default**; example config `maxMemories: 5, maxSuggestions: 3`) to the Copilot CLI prompt's token consumption; credit-spending autospawner daemons (`BROKER_AUTOSPAWN_ENABLED=1`) are off by default, and the README states no measured per-prompt token/credit cost for context injection or for `--evaluator-cwd` prune assessment sessions (F355, F407, F427, F428, F497, F546, F562, F563, F575; see Q15).

- **7. Cross-surface reach:**
  - *Native Copilot Memory + Dreaming:* Broadest native reach across the GitHub ecosystem: **eight named surfaces** (**GitHub Copilot app, Copilot CLI, Copilot code review, Copilot cloud agent, Agentic Autofix, Copilot in GitHub Mobile, Copilot in Slack, and Copilot in Microsoft Teams**) plus **Visual Studio Code** (via `chat.copilot.memory.enabled`) and **Copilot SDK** sessions, with memories captured on one surface automatically reusable on the others (except code review and autofix, which apply repository-level facts only) (F19, F20, F21, F22, F23, F27, F498, F499, F501). Dreaming curates personal and repository memories, proposes pull requests to eight tracked instruction/skill/agent file patterns, and updates internal code review guidance (F143, F144, F145, F164, F503).
  - *In-repo Markdown lessons wiki:* Because `.copilot/lessons/` and `~/.copilot/lessons/` are **not** in Copilot CLI's automatic custom-instructions discovery list (`$HOME/.copilot/copilot-instructions.md`, `$HOME/.copilot/instructions/**/*.instructions.md`, `.github/copilot-instructions.md`, `.github/instructions/**/*.instructions.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `GEMINI.md`, `COPILOT_CUSTOM_INSTRUCTIONS_DIRS`, F533) and are **not** in Dreaming's eight tracked file patterns (F164, F503), raw lesson files reach a surface only when an agent is prompted or instructed to read them (F525, F532). Once `/consolidate-lessons` promotes a durable rule into Layer 3 (`AGENTS.md` or a targeted `.github/instructions/*.instructions.md` file, F522), GitHub's custom-instructions support matrix shows that agent instructions (`AGENTS.md`) and path-specific instructions (`.github/instructions/**/*.instructions.md`) are supported by **Copilot CLI** and, on `github.com`, by **Copilot cloud agent** and **Copilot code review**, but are **not** supported by **Copilot Chat on `github.com`** (which supports personal, repository-wide, and organization instructions only) or by **Copilot code review in VS Code** (which supports repository-wide instructions only) (F522, F533, F534).
  - *SCNS (alpha):* Scoped to **Copilot CLI** via `~/.copilot/extensions/scns/extension.mjs` (plus curl-based `hooks.json` shell hooks for extension-less environments and the `~/.copilot/session-store.db` poller) and projections into `.github/copilot-instructions.md` (F375, F377, F389, F390, F403, F542, F558, F559, F560; while GitHub's extension docs state extensions run in the GitHub Copilot app and Copilot CLI, F573, the SCNS README itself does not mention Copilot Memory, Dreaming, code review, the Copilot app, or VS Code, F574 — see Q16).

- **8. Offline and local operation:**
  - *Native Copilot Memory + Dreaming:* Microsoft's VS Code documentation states that the Copilot harness uses Copilot Memory `"to store repository facts and user preferences in the cloud"` and that `"Repository memories require a GitHub-hosted repository"` (F469; see F469 caveat), while GitHub's concept page states that repository-level facts and user-level preferences are viewed and deleted in repository and user settings on GitHub.com (F12, F13, F14, F468). For Copilot Dreaming, a Copilot conversation does not need to stay open for it to run (F168, F229, F505), and it runs on a schedule managed by GitHub (F160, F227, F483); session syncing — which `"saves local sessions to your GitHub account"` (`remoteExport` default `true` in `~/.copilot/settings.json`; on Business and Enterprise plans requires the `Store local sessions in the Cloud` policy to be at least `View from cloud`, otherwise `"sessions remain local"`) — is **Required** for *Repository customization* and *Personal memory curation* and **Not required** for *Code review customization* (F147, F183, F184, F185, F190, F191, F195, F471, F505). For bulk CLI import, `copilot memories import PATH-TO-FILE --dry-run` validates the complete JSONL file without authenticating or submitting memories, whereas a non-dry-run import submits records individually to the memory service after validation (F280, F281, F290, F506). GitHub's Copilot Memory concept page **does not mention offline operation** (F512), and the Dreaming concept page does not state where Dreaming executes, so offline runtime behavior of native Copilot Memory remains an open question (`NO-SOURCE` on the opened concept page; see Q9 and Q16) (F505, F506, F512; see Q9, Q16).
  - *In-repo Markdown lessons wiki:* **100% local files in git** with zero external servers, databases, or embedding models to install or operate (though running Copilot CLI itself still requires model connectivity) (F515, F526, F536).
  - *SCNS (alpha):* **Local-first runtime** (`127.0.0.1:3456` and `3500–3599`, local `~/.scns/memory/vault.db`, local ONNX embeddings) with no data uploaded anywhere; however, initial installation (`npm ci` native prebuilds) and the **first embedding computation on a fresh install (~128 MB model download into `local_cache/`)** require network connectivity unless `local_cache/` or `SCNS_EMBED_CACHE_DIR` is pre-seeded, and if the local broker is unreachable the CLI extension degrades gracefully into a no-op within its timeout (F302, F344, F345, F378, F392, F424, F426, F539, F540, F559, F565, F566).

- **9. Isolation:**
  - *Native Copilot Memory + Dreaming:* Repository-level facts are isolated **per GitHub repository**, are **not pooled** across an organization's repositories, and are **not copied on `git fork`**; user-level preferences follow the user across repositories by design, and on Business and Enterprise plans are isolated **per billing entity** (retrieved only when that billing entity is active and left behind on license changes) (F5, F12, F13, F67, F111, F112, F507, F508, F509, F510).
  - *In-repo Markdown lessons wiki:* Separates project scope (`.copilot/lessons/`, per repository) from global scope (`~/.copilot/lessons/`, per workstation), but agents consult both and rely on a **prompt-level precedence convention** (global wins on conflict unless the project lesson declares `scope: project-override`, which `/consolidate-lessons` refuses to auto-reconcile) rather than process or database isolation (F513, F514, F518, F519, F537).
  - *SCNS (alpha):* Enforces per-repository isolation structurally via **separate child broker processes on ports `3500–3599` and separate SQLite databases**, resolved once at session start and guarded against silent fallback when the meta-broker dies by setting **`SCNS_REQUIRE_PROJECT_BROKER=1`** (strict routing), supplemented by directory-scoped removal commands that refuse unregistered paths and cross-project pollution audit/repair scripts (F308, F321, F322, F323, F326, F327, F333, F335, F395, F396, F398, F435, F436, F549, F552, F553, F554, F555, F556, F557).

## 5. Execution

The work this specification defines **is** this verified research baseline (`specs/006-copilot-memory-dreaming-scns.md`), executed by the `spec-forge` loop below so that the hand-written lab specification `specs/labs/lab10.md` can cite its `F<n>` rows during the downstream `lab-forge` rewrite of `labs/lab10.md` (where any downstream stage or agent not fixed here is **To be assigned by John**).

| Stage | Agent | Contract — what it is given, what it must return |
|---|---|---|
| 1 — `scope` | `general-purpose` | Decomposes the question into independently researchable sub-questions and asserts nothing |
| 2 — `gather` | `research` | One researcher per sub-question; returns claims with a primary-source URL and date, or states that nothing is published |
| 3 — `verify` | `Khriss` | Is told to open every cited source itself and grade each claim `VERIFIED`, `UNVERIFIED`, `CONTRADICTED` or `NO-SOURCE`; never sees researcher prose |
| 4 — `author` | `Vasher` | Writes the spec from `VERIFIED` claims only; its `§3` table is checked against the ledger by code |
| 5 — `review` | `judge` | Re-derives the acceptance criteria from the objective and tries to break the spec; never sees the author's summary |

**Loop:** `spec-forge`  **maxRounds:** `2`  **Escalates to:** John

---

## 6. Model assignment

Assignment is by **stage property**, not stage prestige. The table below records the `declaredModel` that the `spec-forge` loop requests for each stage; the model that actually served each stage is recorded in the run result (`modelsUsed`) and can differ (for any downstream work outside `spec-forge`, model assignment is **To be assigned by John**).

| Stage | Model | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| `scope` | `gemini-4-argon` | Decomposes the research objective into bounded, independent sub-questions without asserting unverified facts |
| `gather` | `claude-sonnet-5.5` | Parallel primary-source gathering per sub-question; every returned claim is independently re-opened downstream |
| `verify` | `gpt-6.1-sol` | Independent primary-source adjudication across web URLs and approved grounding files without seeing researcher prose |
| `author` | `gemini-4-argon` | Synthesizes the specification strictly from `VERIFIED` rows across a large context window |
| `review` | `claude-opus-5.5` | Adversarial judgment, load-bearing, re-derives acceptance criteria from the objective and audits claims against primary sources |

> Workflow-owned subagents are **not** subject to the `task`-tool sub-agent model gate.
> Per-stage model assignment inside a loop is free.
